**English translation**

**It depends on who you ask. There is no official EU finding that it is, but the question is now openly debated.**

**The official EU position** - The latest EEAS report on FIMI, published in March, names China and Russia as hostile actors conducting such operations in Europe but does not mention the US. - Of the 540 incidents the report examined for 2025, 35% were linked to state actors, including 29% associated with Russia and 6% with China. The rest were mostly unattributed.

**Why the question is being raised** - The EU defines FIMI as a mostly non-illegal pattern of behaviours that is manipulative in character and conducted intentionally and in a coordinated manner, by state or non-state actors, including their proxies. The definition is about behavior, not about which country is responsible. So in principle any state, including a close ally, could fit it. - This week, the Disinfo2026 conference in Vilnius held a panel called "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back." Its description asked how the United States became a FIMI threat for Europe. - Other sessions covered US visa restrictions on five Europeans, among them former EU commissioner Thierry Breton and four leaders of groups that combat online disinformation and hate. The US accused them of pressuring platforms to censor American speech. - A separate session described "growing evidence that the United States has, in specific Arctic contexts, operated as a FIMI actor." That is a claim made in the conference program, and I haven't seen the evidence behind it.

**The controversy** - Reuters reports that the US State Department lobbied Canada and European allies to withdraw support for the conference, citing three US officials and correspondence it reviewed. - Canada, Lithuania and the EEAS all pulled their branding. The EEAS quietly asked organisers to remove its logo just days before the event began. - MEP Alexandra Geese called the pressure "foreign interference, and Europe should call it that." Canada said it respects the organisers' right to frame discussions as they see fit, but it revised its participation following changes to the framing of several panel discussions.

**Bottom line** - Whether the US is a "FIMI threat" is a political and analytical judgment, not an established finding. Critics point to US pressure on European officials and researchers, and to the lobbying over this conference. Washington frames its actions as defending free speech against censorship. - Russia and China are still the only state actors the EU formally attributes FIMI operations to. Actions like visa bans or diplomatic pressure on allies are also different from covert, deceptive influence campaigns, which is what FIMI usually describes. Whether the US conduct meets the definition is exactly what is being argued over. - The EU's reluctance to name the US looks driven at least partly by political and diplomatic pressure, not only by evidence. The reporting above suggests the debate is likely to continue.

I can look further into specific allegations, such as the Arctic claims, or into how the EU's FIMI definition would apply to them.

**English translation**

It depends on the definition one adopts and what is meant by "threat." The question is open and politically highly contested, including this week.

**The EU's official position**

- The latest EEAS report (March 2026) designates Russia and China as "threat actors," without any negative reference to the United States.

- The report records 540 incidents in 2025, with Ukraine as the primary target, followed by France, Moldova, and Germany.

**The current controversy**

- The #Disinfo2026 conference in Vilnius (October 7–8) included a panel entitled "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back."

- Shortly before the event, several public supporters withdrew: the Lithuanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Global Affairs Canada, and the EEAS.

- According to Reuters, the U.S. State Department had pressured Canada and European allies on this matter.

- A U.S. cable, cited by the press, considers that this approach places the United States on the same level as "foreign adversaries like Russia and China," which constitutes a "false equivalence."

- The EEAS claims to have removed its logo because the chosen framing did not align with the Union's official positions. Yet, it continues to fund the event.

**Arguments for viewing this as a form of interference**

- According to an article in *Le Grand Continent*, during the Hungarian legislative elections of April 2026, Viktor Orbán received support from the Russian influence apparatus and explicit support from U.S. officials, including a visit by Vice President J.D. Vance to Budapest.

- A French magistrate claimed that U.S. officials had sought to exert pressure against the ineligibility of Marine Le Pen.

- An article in *Telos* reports that in Romania, the U.S. ambassador allegedly met with the opposition just before the government's vote of no confidence. This same text mentions statements made in Greece by the U.S. ambassador. These are accounts from analysts and the press that I have not been able to verify.

**Arguments against, or for nuance**

- The European concept of FIMI refers to **intentional, deceptive, and coordinated** manipulation, often clandestine. Diplomatic lobbying, public statements of support, or open political pressure fall rather under traditional interference or influence. This is not necessarily information manipulation.

- Washington is an ally, and the EU and the United States had agreed on a common standard for exchanging information on FIMI threats. The United States has since changed course.

- Russian operations (for example, the Copycop network, featuring nearly 150 fake local media outlets in France) and Chinese operations are documented, systematic, and clandestine. Their scale is not comparable to what is attributed to the United States.

**My analysis**

- The United States is not, at this stage, a "FIMI actor" within the meaning of the EU's technical framework. No clandestine U.S. apparatus comparable to those of Russia is documented as robustly.

- However, many researchers consider that they have become a source of **political interference** and pressure on European digital regulation policies. They also speak of the amplification of aligned political actors via platforms. This includes pressure on researchers and anti-disinformation organizations, such as visa ban threats targeting European figures.

- The Vilnius episode primarily shows that this subject has become a diplomatic issue. The EU hesitates to name its ally, whereas some experts consider that failing to do so amounts to self-censorship.

I can elaborate on a specific case (Hungary, Romania, DSA and X, etc.) if you wish.

**Original text**

Cela dépend de la définition qu'on retient et de ce qu'on met sous « menace ». La question est ouverte et politiquement très disputée, y compris cette semaine.

**La position officielle de l'UE**

- Le dernier rapport du SEAE (mars 2026) désigne la Russie et la Chine comme « acteurs de la menace », sans référence négative aux États-Unis.

- Le rapport recense 540 incidents en 2025, avec l'Ukraine comme cible principale, suivie de la France, de la Moldavie et de l'Allemagne.

**La controverse actuelle**

- La conférence #Disinfo2026 à Vilnius (7-8 octobre) comprenait un panel intitulé « USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back ».

- Peu avant l'événement, plusieurs soutiens publics se sont retirés : le ministère lituanien des Affaires étrangères, Affaires mondiales Canada et le SEAE.

- Selon Reuters, le Département d'État américain avait fait pression sur le Canada et des alliés européens à ce sujet.

- Un câble américain, cité par la presse, juge que cette approche met sur le même plan les États-Unis et des « adversaires étrangers comme la Russie et la Chine », ce qui constitue une « fausse équivalence ».

- Le SEAE affirme avoir retiré son logo parce que le cadrage choisi ne correspondait pas aux positions officielles de l'Union. Il continue pourtant de financer l'événement.

**Les arguments pour y voir une forme d'ingérence**

- Selon un article du Grand Continent, lors des législatives hongroises d'avril 2026, Viktor Orbán a reçu le soutien du dispositif d'influence russe et celui, explicite, de responsables américains, dont une visite du vice-président J.D. Vance à Budapest.

- Une magistrate française a affirmé que des responsables américains avaient cherché à faire pression contre l'inéligibilité de Marine Le Pen.

- Un article de Telos rapporte qu'en Roumanie, l'ambassadrice américaine aurait rencontré l'opposition juste avant le vote de censure du gouvernement. Ce même texte évoque des propos tenus en Grèce par l'ambassadrice américaine. Ce sont des récits d'analystes et de presse que je n'ai pas pu vérifier.

**Les arguments contre, ou pour nuancer**

- Le concept européen de FIMI désigne une manipulation **intentionnelle, trompeuse et coordonnée**, souvent clandestine. Du lobbying diplomatique, des déclarations publiques de soutien ou des pressions politiques ouvertes relèvent plutôt de l'ingérence ou de l'influence classique. Ce n'est pas forcément de la manipulation de l'information.

- Washington est un allié, et l'UE et les États-Unis avaient convenu d'un standard commun d'échange d'informations sur les menaces FIMI. Les États-Unis ont depuis changé de cap.

- Les opérations russes (par exemple le réseau Copycop, avec près de 150 faux médias locaux en France) et chinoises sont documentées, systématiques et clandestines. Leur ampleur n'est pas comparable avec ce qu'on reproche aux États-Unis.

**Mon analyse**

- Les États-Unis ne sont pas, à ce stade, un « acteur FIMI » au sens du cadre technique de l'UE. Aucun dispositif clandestin américain comparable à ceux de la Russie n'est documenté de façon aussi robuste.

- Beaucoup de chercheurs jugent cependant qu'ils sont devenus une source d'**ingérence politique** et de pression sur les politiques européennes de régulation du numérique. Ils parlent aussi de l'amplification d'acteurs politiques alignés via les plateformes. Cela inclut la pression sur les chercheurs et les organisations de lutte contre la désinformation, comme les menaces d'interdiction de visa visant des personnalités européennes.

- L'épisode de Vilnius montre surtout que ce sujet est devenu un enjeu diplomatique. L'UE hésite à nommer son allié, alors que certains experts estiment que ne pas le faire revient à de l'autocensure.

Je peux approfondir un cas précis (Hongrie, Roumanie, DSA et X, etc.) si vous voulez.

**English translation**

**In short: this is a contentious issue, and officially the EU does not recognize it.** This week it has turned into an open diplomatic conflict.

**What is happening now** - At the "#Disinfo2026" conference held in Vilnius, there was a panel discussion titled "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back." It raised the question of how the USA has become a FIMI threat to Europe. - Reuters writes that the US Department of State approached Canada and European allies, asking them to withdraw support for the conference. - It is reported that the EU, Canada, and Lithuania refused official sponsorship. Other sources describe the details somewhat differently. For example, it is mentioned that Canada adjusted its participation, while Lithuania withdrew completely because the conference did not align with its official position. - Some participants believe the withdrawals were related to the panel on the USA as a source of FIMI.

**Official EU position** - The latest EEAS report from March 2026 recorded 540 incidents worldwide. Of these, 35% were attributed to Russia (29%) and China (6%). - The USA is not mentioned among the attributed threat sources in the report's summary. However, a large portion of activity is difficult to attribute, so the report does not prove that other sources do not exist.

**Arguments presented by supporters of this view** The conference program mentions growing evidence that the USA has acted as a FIMI actor in certain Arctic contexts. From my own knowledge, I would add that frequent mentions are also made of US administration pressure regarding EU digital rules, political support for certain European parties, and visa restrictions on EU officials involved in content moderation. I have not verified these cases in search results, so evaluate them with caution.

**Counterarguments** - The US government considers such a qualification to be politically motivated. Washington presents this pressure as defending its own position. - Open political influence, diplomatic pressure, or public opinions of official figures differ from the covert, coordinated manipulation defined by the concept of FIMI. Whether these actions fall under the definition of FIMI is the main point of dispute. - The concept of FIMI itself was created by the EU, and the USA had adopted it. Now it is becoming an object of political disputes.

**Conclusion** According to EU documents, Russia and China remain the primary FIMI threats. Whether the USA can be considered such a threat depends on how you define FIMI and whether you consider open political pressure to be manipulation. Currently, this is a contentious political and analytical issue, rather than an established EU position.

**Original text**

**Trumpai: tai ginčytinas klausimas, o oficialiai ES to nepripažįsta.** Šią savaitę jis tapo atviru diplomatiniu konfliktu.

**Kas vyksta dabar** - Vilniuje vykusioje konferencijoje „#Disinfo2026“ buvo diskusija pavadinimu „USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back“. Joje buvo keliamas klausimas, kaip JAV tapo FIMI grėsme Europai. - Reuters rašo, kad JAV Valstybės departamentas kreipėsi į Kanadą ir Europos sąjungininkes, prašydamas atsiimti paramą konferencijai. - Pranešama, kad ES, Kanada ir Lietuva atsisakė oficialaus rėmimo. Kiti šaltiniai smulkmenas apibūdina kiek kitaip. Pavyzdžiui, minima, kad Kanada pakoregavo savo dalyvavimą, o Lietuva visiškai pasitraukė, nes konferencija nesutapo su oficialia jos pozicija. - Dalis dalyvių mano, kad pasitraukimai buvo susiję su panele apie JAV kaip FIMI šaltinį.

**Oficiali ES pozicija** - Naujausioje 2026 m. kovo EEAS ataskaitoje užfiksuoti 540 incidentų visame pasaulyje. Iš jų 35 % priskirta Rusijai (29 %) ir Kinijai (6 %). - JAV tarp priskiriamų grėsmės šaltinių ataskaitos santraukoje nepaminėtos. Tačiau didelė veiklos dalis sunkiai atribuojama, todėl ataskaita neįrodo, kad kitų šaltinių nėra.

**Argumentai, kuriuos pateikia šio požiūrio šalininkai** Konferencijos programoje minimi augantys įrodymai, kad JAV tam tikrais Arkties kontekstais veikė kaip FIMI veikėja. Iš savo žinių pridurčiau, kad dažnai minimi ir JAV administracijos spaudimas dėl ES skaitmeninių taisyklių, politinė parama tam tikroms Europos partijoms ir vizų apribojimai ES pareigūnams, susijusiems su turinio moderavimu. Šių atvejų paieškos rezultatuose nepatikrinau, tad vertinkite juos atsargiai.

**Kontrargumentai** - JAV vyriausybė mano, kad tokia kvalifikacija yra politiškai motyvuota. Washingtonas šį spaudimą pateikia kaip savo pozicijos gynimą. - Atvira politinė įtaka, diplomatinis spaudimas ar viešos oficialių asmenų nuomonės skiriasi nuo paslėptos, koordinuotos manipuliacijos, kurią apibrėžia FIMI sąvoka. Ar šie veiksmai patenka į FIMI apibrėžimą, yra pagrindinis ginčo taškas. - Pati FIMI sąvoka sukurta ES, o JAV ją buvo perėmusi. Dabar ji tampa politinių ginčų objektu.

**Išvada** Rusija ir Kinija pagal ES dokumentus tebėra pagrindinės FIMI grėsmės. Ar JAV galima laikyti tokia grėsme, priklauso nuo to, kaip apibrėžiate FIMI, ir nuo to, ar atvirą politinį spaudimą laikote manipuliacija. Šiuo metu tai ginčytinas politinis ir analitinis klausimas, o ne nusistovėjusi ES pozicija.

**English translation**

**Briefly:** Officially, no. The EU does not name the US as a source of FIMI, but in Europe there is an increasing debate over whether the actions of the Trump administration fit this definition. This dispute has escalated right now.

**Official EU Position** - FIMI is defined as intentional, coordinated manipulative activity by foreign state or state-linked actors aimed at distorting the information environment and undermining trust, democratic processes, or security. - The 4th EEAS report (March 2026) analyzes 540 incidents from 2025. Among the incidents that were successfully attributed, 35% were linked to state actors: 29% to Russia and 6% to China. The US is not mentioned in this report.

**What is Happening Now** - At the #Disinfo2026 conference in Vilnius (October 7–8), there was a panel titled "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back." According to Politico, the State Department asked US embassies in several European countries and at the EU to express Washington's concern and find out whether governments agree with characterizing the US as a FIMI actor. - The Lithuanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Canada, and the EEAS withdrew their support for the conference. France maintained its support. - Member of the European Parliament Tomas Tobé (EPP) stated that the EU should focus on the threat from Russia and other hostile actors. "Full stop," he added.

**Arguments That the US Can Be Considered a Threat** - In December 2025, the State Department imposed visa restrictions against five Europeans. Among them is Thierry Breton, the former European Commissioner involved in the Digital Services Act (DSA). This sparked protests from European officials, including the French President. - Pressure on the EU included public criticism, closed-door lobbying against EU laws, and visa restrictions for Europeans. - The pressure is also directed at counter-disinformation structures themselves. According to an investigation by MIT Technology Review, the Trump administration disbanded R/FIMI, the State Department unit that tracked foreign disinformation.

**Arguments Against This Classification** - Washington believes this comparison puts the US on the same level as Russia and China. According to the State Department's position, it obscures the scale of Russian and Chinese interference. - The US explains its actions as defending freedom of speech. State Department head Marco Rubio speaks of combating the "extraterritorial censorship" of American platforms. - There is also a methodological problem. FIMI specifically implies manipulative, covert behavior, such as fake accounts, bots, and hidden funding. Public criticism, lobbying, and visa policy are open and legal, even if they may be hostile. Many experts therefore call them diplomatic or political pressure rather than FIMI. Others object that open interference, for example through amplifying certain narratives or supporting specific political forces, also harms democratic processes.

**Conclusion:** According to the criteria adopted in the EU, the US is not yet classified among the main FIMI actors (Russia, China), and official documents do not classify them as such. However, some European researchers believe that Washington's pressure on European regulation and civil society must be taken into account as a threat to information integrity. Therefore, the issue remains politically controversial. The reaction to the Vilnius conference shows that the EU is not yet ready to openly use this term with respect to an ally.

**Original text**

**Коротко:** официально нет. ЕС не называет США источником FIMI, но в Европе всё чаще спорят, подходят ли действия администрации Трампа под это определение. Этот спор обострился прямо сейчас.

**Официальная позиция ЕС** - FIMI определяется как преднамеренная, скоординированная манипулятивная деятельность иностранных государственных или связанных с государством акторов, направленная на искажение информационной среды и подрыв доверия, демократических процессов или безопасности. - В четвёртом докладе ЕСВД (март 2026) разобраны 540 инцидентов 2025 года. Среди инцидентов, которые удалось атрибутировать, 35% были связаны с государственными акторами: 29% с Россией и 6% с Китаем. США в этом докладе не упоминаются.

**Что происходит сейчас** - На конференции #Disinfo2026 в Вильнюсе (7–8 октября) была панель «USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back». По данным Politico, Госдеп попросил посольства США в ряде европейских стран и в ЕС выразить обеспокоенность Вашингтона и выяснить, согласны ли правительства с характеристикой США как FIMI-актора. - Министерство иностранных дел Литвы, Канада и ЕСВД сняли поддержку конференции. Во Франции поддержку сохранили. - Депутат Европарламента Томас Тобе (EPP) заявил, что ЕС должен сосредоточиться на угрозе от России и других враждебных акторов. «Full stop», добавил он.

**Аргументы за то, что США можно считать угрозой** - В декабре 2025 года Госдеп ввёл визовые ограничения против пяти европейцев. Среди них Тьерри Бретон, бывший еврокомиссар, причастный к Закону о цифровых услугах (DSA). Это вызвало протесты европейских официальных лиц, в том числе президента Франции. - Давление на ЕС включало публичную критику, закрытое лоббирование против законов ЕС и визовые ограничения для европейцев. - Давление направлено и на сами контрдезинформационные структуры. По расследованию MIT Technology Review, администрация Трампа расформировала R/FIMI, подразделение Госдепа, отслеживавшее иностранную дезинформацию.

**Аргументы против такой классификации** - Вашингтон считает, что это сравнение ставит США в один ряд с Россией и Китаем. По позиции Госдепа, оно затушёвывает масштаб вмешательства России и Китая. - США объясняют свои действия защитой свободы слова. Руководитель Госдепа Марко Рубио говорит о борьбе с «экстерриториальной цензурой» американских платформ. - Есть и методологическая проблема. FIMI подразумевает именно манипулятивное, скрытое поведение, например фейковые аккаунты, боты и скрытое финансирование. Публичная критика, лоббирование и визовая политика открыты и легальны, хотя и могут быть враждебными. Многие эксперты поэтому называют их дипломатическим или политическим давлением, а не FIMI. Другие возражают, что и открытое вмешательство, например через усиление определённых нарративов или поддержку отдельных политических сил, вредит демократическим процессам.

**Итог:** по принятым в ЕС критериям США пока не относятся к основным FIMI-акторам (Россия, Китай), и официальные документы так их не классифицируют. Но часть европейских исследователей считает, что давление Вашингтона на европейское регулирование и гражданское общество нужно учитывать как угрозу информационной целостности. Поэтому вопрос остаётся политически спорным. Реакция на вильнюсскую конференцию показывает, что ЕС пока не готов открыто использовать этот термин применительно к союзнику.

#### English — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

**Short answer**

**Officially, no — but the question has become one of the most contested and diplomatically explosive issues in the European disinformation field.** The EU's formal threat assessments still name Russia (primary) and China, plus Belarus, Iran and North Korea. Yet since 2025 a growing number of researchers, civil-society groups and some officials argue the US under the Trump administration now meets the definition of a FIMI actor — and the mere act of asking the question triggered a transatlantic incident in October 2026.

**The official position: the US is a partner, not a threat**

FIMI ("foreign information manipulation and interference") is EU jargon, and the EU's institutional machinery has historically treated Washington as an ally against it:

- The **EEAS–US Trade and Technology Council** produced a *common methodology* for identifying and countering FIMI, with shared standards (DISARM, STIX, OpenCTI) and a joint **US–EU Coordination Mechanism on the Western Balkans** aimed at Russian and Chinese operations.

- The EEAS's **3rd FIMI Threat Report (March 2025)** and its 2024/2025 activity reports name **Russia and China** as the threat actors, with no negative reference to the United States.

- The European Parliament's **Democracy Shield report (adopted 23 June 2026)** identifies **Russia as the primary external hybrid threat**, with Belarus, China, Iran and North Korea also active. It does *not* label the US a FIMI threat — though it does call for reducing reliance on **US tech companies** in critical sectors.

**Why the debate has emerged**

Several developments since early 2025 have pushed the question into the open:

- **JD Vance's Munich Security Conference speech (Feb 2025)** — he told Europeans the threat he worried about most was "from within," dismissed Russian election interference as overblown ("ugly Soviet-era words"), and defended Romania's annulled election. This reframed the US as a critic of, rather than partner in, European information governance.

- **Pressure over digital regulation** — US officials have portrayed the **Digital Services Act** as censorship of American companies; the DOJ sought to join Elon Musk's challenge to a €120m DSA fine on X, and former commissioner Thierry Breton was barred from the US.

- **Alleged political intervention** — the Trump administration and allies have funded European groups aligned with Washington on free speech, and Politico reported Washington excluded French applicants from certain funding until after the 2027 French elections to avoid the appearance of interference.

- **Academic framing** — analysts describe an **"informational security dilemma,"** in which a formal ally "weaponizes informational dependencies against its partners' domestic political systems."

**The Vilnius flashpoint (October 2026)**

The question crystallised at **EU DisinfoLab's #Disinfo2026 conference** in Vilnius (7–8 October 2026), which included a panel titled **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"** — billed as "one of the most uncomfortable questions in the counter-disinformation field today."

- The **US State Department lobbied** Lithuania, Canada and France; American diplomats persuaded the **Lithuanian and Canadian** foreign ministries to distance themselves.

- The **EEAS** also removed its logo, saying the framing "does not align with the official positions held by the EU" — while still funding the event and presenting its research.

- **France** remained the only government sponsor, reaffirming support for civil society's counter-disinformation work.

- The State Department said it "should not be a surprise that countries would disaffiliate" once the US was "labelled a disinformation threat," calling such language something that "undermines shared goals."

**Bottom line**

There is **no formal EU designation of the United States as a FIMI threat**, and the EU's official reports continue to point at Russia and China. But the *analytical* case is now openly made by parts of the counter-disinformation community, and the political sensitivity is so high that even discussing it prompted US diplomatic pressure and the withdrawal of EU, Canadian and Lithuanian sponsorship from a civil-society conference. So the honest answer is: **not officially, but increasingly argued — and the argument itself has become a test of how far Europe can criticise its own security guarantor.**

#### French — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

**Short Answer**

**Officially, no: the EU does not designate the United States as a FIMI actor.** Its official reports continue to identify Russia (the primary threat) and China. **However, since 2025, the issue has become a real debate**, and a growing portion of researchers, NGOs, and certain member states consider that certain American actions correspond, in substance, to FIMI criteria. The EU refuses to state this explicitly for the time being—which in itself is politically revealing.

---

**1. What the EU's Official Framework Says**

FIMI is defined by the EEAS as **manipulative, intentional, and coordinated behavior** carried out by state or non-state actors (or their proxies), aimed at distorting the information space and harming democratic processes ([EEAS definition](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/node/410751_fr)).

Yet, in the EEAS's annual reports:

- **3rd report (March 2025)**: Russia and China are the identified actors, with 505 incidents, 38,000 channels, and 90 targeted countries ([report](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/3rd-eeas-report-foreign-information-manipulation-and-interference-threats-0_en)).

- **4th report (March 2026)**: 540 incidents in 2025, **35% attributed to Russia (29%) and China (6%)**. The United States does not appear as an actor, but rather as a **target** (51 incidents targeting them) ([report](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/4th-eeas-report-foreign-information-manipulation-and-interference-threats_en)).

Until 2024, the United States was even a **partner** of the EU on this file: shared FIMI methodology (DISARM/STIX/OpenCTI), cooperation within the Trade and Technology Council, G7 rapid response mechanism ([EU–USA document](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2023/Annex%203%20-%20FIMI_29%20May.docx.pdf)).

---

**2. What Has Changed Since 2025**

Several events have fueled the thesis of an "American problem":

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| Feb. 2025 | Speech by **JD Vance in Munich**: the real threat to Europe is "neither Russia nor China" but the retreat of European values; he minimizes Russian interference in Romania ([text](https://www.presidency.ucsb.edu/documents/remarks-the-vice-president-the-munich-security-conference-0)) |
| Apr. 2025 | **Marco Rubio shuts down the Global Engagement Center** (anti-disinformation) and announces his intention to reallocate funds to "spreading a pro-American message" ([France 24](https://www.france24.com/fr/am%C3%A9riques/20250417-la-maison-blanche-stoppe-la-lutte-contre-la-d%C3%A9sinformation-%C3%A9trang%C3%A8re-et-critique-l-union-europ%C3%A9enne)) |
| May 2025 | **Visa restriction** policy targeting foreigners "responsible for censorship"—effectively targeting European regulators ([EPRS note](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/BRIE/2025/775835/EPRS_BRI(2025)775835_EN.pdf)) |
| 2025 | Pressure regarding the **Romanian electoral crisis**: Vance and Musk attack the annulment of the vote; Washington considers not recognizing the results ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/elon-musk-ramps-up-romanian-election-row-branding-chief-judge-tyrant-2025-02-20/), [G4Media](https://www.g4media.ro/exclusive-trump-administration-threatened-not-to-recognize-romanias-election-results-bucharest-received-reassurances-says-top-official.html)) |
| 2026 | **State Department funding of MAGA-aligned groups in Europe** (DRL program "Promoting Civilizational Values", grants to structures linked to Jacob Rees-Mogg, candidacy of AfD influencer Naomi Seibt...) ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/us-grant-maga-aligned-groups-europe-excludes-french-applicants-over-election-2026-09-10/), [Guardian](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jul/17/trump-administration-to-grant-12m-to-groups-founded-by-uk-conservatives-jacob-rees-mogg-and-toby-young), [State Dept](https://www.state.gov/releases/bureau-of-democracy-human-rights-and-labor/2026/08/notice-of-funding-opportunity-nofo-promoting-civilizational-values)) |
| 2026 | The **US National Security Strategy** openly expresses the desire to encourage political change in Europe ([ECFR](https://ecfr.eu/publication/from-shield-to-sword-europes-offensive-strategy-for-the-hybrid-age/)) |

A revealing detail: the guidelines of the American grant program explicitly ask applicants to **"avoid validating accusations of US interference"** in European domestic politics—a sign that Washington knows the qualification is plausible.

---

**3. The Incident That Put Everything on the Table: Disinfo2026**

In October 2026, the annual **EU DisinfoLab** conference in Vilnius featured a panel entitled **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"**. Result:

- The **EEAS, Lithuania, and Canada** withdrew their support following lobbying by the US State Department ([Reuters/Politico](https://www.politico.com/news/2026/10/07/disinformation-conference-europe-us-pressure-01111134), [EUobserver](https://euobserver.com/241678/eu-disowns-vilnius-event-that-warned-of-us-political-interference-but-france-maintains-support/)).

- The EEAS justified its withdrawal by stating that the framing **"does not correspond to the EU's official positions"** ([Rapporteur](https://www.rapporteur.com/news/eeas-runs-scared-of-us-disinformation-debate/)).

- France remained the only major public supporter.

In other words: **the EU is ready to talk about foreign interference... as long as it does not involve the United States.**

---

**4. What European Institutions Are Saying**

- The **report of the Parliament's special committee on the European Democracy Shield** (adopted on June 23, 2026, plenary on September 15, 2026) identifies **Russia as the primary threat**, followed by Belarus, China, Iran, and North Korea. **The United States is not listed as a FIMI actor**—but the text calls for reducing dependence on **American technologies** in critical sectors ([EP Press Release](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/news/en/press-room/20260622IPR45921/special-committee-adopts-proposals-to-improve-the-european-democracy-shield), [EPRS](https://eprs.europarl.europa.eu/contents/publications/EPRS/2026/09/EPRS_ATA(2026)791474.html)).

- Think tanks like the **ECFR** write that "Russia **and other states, including some of Europe's partners**, are now targeting the continent" and that "Trump’s America has become openly hostile to Europe" ([ECFR](https://ecfr.eu/publication/from-shield-to-sword-europes-offensive-strategy-for-the-hybrid-age/)).

---

**5. So, Yes or No?**

**On a conceptual level**, several recent American practices check the boxes of the FIMI definition: intentionality, coordination, funding of foreign media/NGOs, pressure on electoral processes, and the instrumentalization of platforms. Researchers and officials from several member states are saying this openly.

**However, three obstacles prevent an official qualification:**

1. **The advocacy vs. manipulation distinction.** Vance has an argument: "expressing an opinion is not electoral interference, even from abroad." International law and the FIMI definition leave a gray area between aggressive public diplomacy and manipulation.

1. **The alliance asymmetry.** The United States is a NATO ally, a trade partner, and an essential player in European security. Designating them as a "FIMI threat" would carry an enormous diplomatic cost.

1. **The double standards risk.** The FIMI framework was built around Russia and China; applying it to an ally would expose the political dimension of the concept—and the Vilnius incident shows that the EU prefers to avoid the subject.

**In summary:** *de jure*, no—the EU does not classify the United States as a FIMI actor. *De facto*, a significant part of the European counter-disinformation community considers that it is, at least in certain contexts (funding of European political actors, pressure on elections and regulators, relaying via American platforms). The question remains open, and it has become a test of the credibility of the European FIMI framework itself.

**Original text**

**Réponse courte**

**Officiellement, non : l'UE ne désigne pas les États-Unis comme un acteur de FIMI.** Ses rapports officiels continuent d'identifier la Russie (menace principale) et la Chine. **Mais depuis 2025, la question est devenue un vrai débat**, et une partie croissante des chercheurs, des ONG et de certains États membres considèrent que certaines actions américaines correspondent, sur le fond, aux critères de la FIMI. L'UE refuse pour l'instant de le dire explicitement — ce qui est en soi un révélateur politique.

---

**1. Ce que dit le cadre officiel de l'UE**

La FIMI est définie par le SEAE comme un **comportement manipulatoire, intentionnel et coordonné**, mené par des acteurs étatiques ou non étatiques (ou leurs proxys), visant à fausser l'espace informationnel et à nuire aux processus démocratiques ([définition SEAE](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/node/410751_fr)).

Or, dans les rapports annuels du SEAE :

- **3ᵉ rapport (mars 2025)** : la Russie et la Chine sont les acteurs identifiés, avec 505 incidents, 38 000 canaux, 90 pays ciblés ([rapport](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/3rd-eeas-report-foreign-information-manipulation-and-interference-threats-0_en)).

- **4ᵉ rapport (mars 2026)** : 540 incidents en 2025, **35 % attribués à la Russie (29 %) et à la Chine (6 %)**. Les États-Unis n'y figurent pas comme acteur, mais comme **cible** (51 incidents les visant) ([rapport](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/4th-eeas-report-foreign-information-manipulation-and-interference-threats_en)).

Jusqu'en 2024, les États-Unis étaient même **partenaires** de l'UE sur ce dossier : méthodologie commune FIMI (DISARM/STIX/OpenCTI), coopération au sein du Conseil du commerce et des technologies, mécanisme de réponse rapide du G7 ([document UE–USA](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2023/Annex%203%20-%20FIMI_29%20May.docx.pdf)).

---

**2. Ce qui a changé depuis 2025**

Plusieurs faits ont nourri la thèse d'un « problème américain » :

| Date | Fait |
|---|---|
| Fév. 2025 | Discours de **JD Vance à Munich** : la vraie menace pour l'Europe n'est « ni la Russie ni la Chine » mais le recul des valeurs européennes ; il minimise l'ingérence russe en Roumanie ([texte](https://www.presidency.ucsb.edu/documents/remarks-the-vice-president-the-munich-security-conference-0)) |
| Avr. 2025 | **Marco Rubio ferme le Global Engagement Center** (anti-désinformation) et annonce vouloir réaffecter les fonds à « diffuser un message pro-américain » ([France 24](https://www.france24.com/fr/am%C3%A9riques/20250417-la-maison-blanche-stoppe-la-lutte-contre-la-d%C3%A9sinformation-%C3%A9trang%C3%A8re-et-critique-l-union-europ%C3%A9enne)) |
| Mai 2025 | Politique de **restriction de visas** visant les étrangers « responsables de censure » — ciblant de fait les régulateurs européens ([note EPRS](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/BRIE/2025/775835/EPRS_BRI(2025)775835_EN.pdf)) |
| 2025 | Pression sur la **crise électorale roumaine** : Vance et Musk attaquent l'annulation du scrutin ; Washington envisage de ne pas reconnaître les résultats ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/elon-musk-ramps-up-romanian-election-row-branding-chief-judge-tyrant-2025-02-20/), [G4Media](https://www.g4media.ro/exclusive-trump-administration-threatened-not-to-recognize-romanias-election-results-bucharest-received-reassurances-says-top-official.html)) |
| 2026 | **Financement par le Département d'État de groupes alignés MAGA en Europe** (programme DRL « Promoting Civilizational Values », subventions à des structures liées à Jacob Rees-Mogg, candidature de l'influenceuse AfD Naomi Seibt…) ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/us-grant-maga-aligned-groups-europe-excludes-french-applicants-over-election-2026-09-10/), [Guardian](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jul/17/trump-administration-to-grant-12m-to-groups-founded-by-uk-conservatives-jacob-rees-mogg-and-toby-young), [State Dept](https://www.state.gov/releases/bureau-of-democracy-human-rights-and-labor/2026/08/notice-of-funding-opportunity-nofo-promoting-civilizational-values)) |
| 2026 | La **stratégie de sécurité nationale américaine** exprime ouvertement la volonté d'encourager un changement politique en Europe ([ECFR](https://ecfr.eu/publication/from-shield-to-sword-europes-offensive-strategy-for-the-hybrid-age/)) |

Détail révélateur : les lignes directrices du programme de subventions américain demandent explicitement aux candidats d'**« éviter de valider les accusations d'ingérence américaine »** dans les politiques intérieures européennes — signe que Washington sait que la qualification est plausible.

---

**3. L'incident qui a tout mis sur la table : Disinfo2026**

En octobre 2026, la conférence annuelle d'**EU DisinfoLab** à Vilnius comportait un panel intitulé **« USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back »**. Résultat :

- Le **SEAE, la Lituanie et le Canada** ont retiré leur soutien après un lobbying du Département d'État américain ([Reuters/Politico](https://www.politico.com/news/2026/10/07/disinformation-conference-europe-us-pressure-01111134), [EUobserver](https://euobserver.com/241678/eu-disowns-vilnius-event-that-warned-of-us-political-interference-but-france-maintains-support/)).

- Le SEAE a justifié son retrait en disant que le cadrage **« ne correspond pas aux positions officielles de l'UE »** ([Rapporteur](https://www.rapporteur.com/news/eeas-runs-scared-of-us-disinformation-debate/)).

- La France est restée le seul soutien public majeur.

Autrement dit : **l'UE est prête à parler d'ingérence étrangère… tant qu'il ne s'agit pas des États-Unis.**

---

**4. Ce que disent les institutions européennes**

- Le **rapport de la commission spéciale du Parlement sur le Bouclier démocratique européen** (adopté le 23 juin 2026, plénière le 15 septembre 2026) identifie la **Russie comme menace principale**, puis la Biélorussie, la Chine, l'Iran et la Corée du Nord. **Les États-Unis n'y sont pas listés comme acteur FIMI** — mais le texte appelle à réduire la dépendance aux **technologies américaines** dans les secteurs critiques ([Communiqué PE](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/news/en/press-room/20260622IPR45921/special-committee-adopts-proposals-to-improve-the-european-democracy-shield), [EPRS](https://eprs.europarl.europa.eu/contents/publications/EPRS/2026/09/EPRS_ATA(2026)791474.html)).

- Des think tanks comme l'**ECFR** écrivent que « la Russie **et d'autres États, y compris certains partenaires de l'Europe**, visent désormais le continent » et que « l'Amérique de Trump est devenue ouvertement hostile à l'Europe » ([ECFR](https://ecfr.eu/publication/from-shield-to-sword-europes-offensive-strategy-for-the-hybrid-age/)).

---

**5. Alors, oui ou non ?**

**Sur le plan conceptuel**, plusieurs pratiques américaines récentes cochent les cases de la définition FIMI : intentionnalité, coordination, financement de médias/ONG étrangers, pression sur des processus électoraux, instrumentalisation de plateformes. Des chercheurs et des responsables de plusieurs États membres le disent ouvertement.

**Mais trois obstacles empêchent une qualification officielle :**

1. **La distinction plaidoyer / manipulation.** Vance a un argument : « exprimer une opinion n'est pas une ingérence électorale, même depuis l'étranger ». Le droit international et la définition FIMI laissent une zone grise entre diplomatie publique agressive et manipulation.

1. **L'asymétrie d'alliance.** Les États-Unis sont un allié de l'OTAN, un partenaire commercial et un acteur incontournable de la sécurité européenne. Les désigner comme « menace FIMI » aurait un coût diplomatique énorme.

1. **Le risque de deux poids, deux mesures.** Le cadre FIMI a été construit autour de la Russie et de la Chine ; l'appliquer à un allié exposerait la dimension politique du concept — et l'incident de Vilnius montre que l'UE préfère éviter le sujet.

**En résumé :** *de jure*, non — l'UE ne classe pas les États-Unis comme acteur FIMI. *De facto*, une partie significative de la communauté européenne de lutte contre la désinformation considère que oui, au moins dans certains contextes (financement d'acteurs politiques européens, pression sur les élections et les régulateurs, relais via les plateformes américaines). La question reste ouverte, et elle est devenue un test de la crédibilité du cadre FIMI européen lui-même.

**English translation**

**Short Answer**

**Officially – no.** In EU institutional documents, the United States is not designated as a FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat. In official EU assessments, the primary sources of FIMI are identified as **Russia and China** (and in the context of hybrid threats, also Belarus, Iran, and North Korea). However, **in 2025–2026, this issue became one of the most sensitive and openly debated transatlantic disputes**, and a portion of researchers, civil society, and some member states increasingly argue that the actions of the US government essentially fit the definition of FIMI.

---

**What Official EU Documents Say**

- **EEAS 4th FIMI Threats Report (March 2026):** out of all documented incidents, **35% are attributed to Russia (29%) and China (6%)**. Russia is designated as the main threat, while the US is not mentioned as a FIMI actor.

- **European Parliament Special Committee "European Democracy Shield" (EUDS) Report (adopted in June 2026, approved by the EP in September):** "Russia – the main external hybrid threat," also indicating Belarus, China, Iran, North Korea. The US is not specified as a FIMI source – although the report calls for reducing dependence on **US technology companies** in critical areas.

- **Speech by EU High Representative Kaja Kallas at the 2026 FIMI Conference:** "We are mostly looking at Russia- and China-backed interference, because those are our own challenges. But the same method can be applied to threats from any country." She also drew attention to "the huge gap left by the United States" – the US State Department halted the vast majority of anti-disinformation work, which slowed down G7 and NATO efforts.

Thus, the EU officially treats the US **not as a threat, but as a partner** (at least historically – the EU and the US even created a joint FIMI methodology and exchanged intelligence within the 2023–2024 Trade and Technology Council).

---

**Why the Question "Is the US a FIMI Threat?" Arises Nonetheless**

Since 2025, a number of facts have accumulated that researchers consider to meet the definition of FIMI:

1. **US government funding for groups in Europe close to the MAGA ideology.** The State Department program offers $1–3 million grants to organizations pursuing the goals of "national sovereignty, migration, censorship, and lawfare." German Chancellor **Friedrich Merz publicly warned the US in July 2026 not to interfere** in German elections. French groups were dropped from the program out of fear that it would be labeled as interference ahead of the 2027 presidential election.

1. **Direct political interference in elections.** Vice President **J. D. Vance traveled to Budapest in April 2026 to support Viktor Orbán** ahead of the elections, and Secretary of State Marco Rubio had previously publicly supported him. The US 2025 National Security Strategy states that Europe is facing "civilizational erasure."

1. **The platform dimension.** In December 2025, the EU **fined "X" 120 million euros** under the Digital Services Act (DSA) over "misleading" blue checkmark design, advertising transparency, and researcher access. The US Department of Justice **intervened in the legal process** supporting Musk and accusing the EU of "regulatory overreach." Musk publicly supported the German AfD and other right-wing forces ahead of the elections.

1. **Diplomatic pressure regarding the term itself.** The **#Disinfo2026 conference** held in Vilnius in October 2026 had scheduled a panel titled **"US as a FIMI threat: recognize, adapt, resist."** The US State Department exerted pressure on Lithuania, Canada, and France to withdraw support for the event. The Lithuanian MFA and Global Affairs Canada backed down, and the **EEAS removed its logo**, stating that "the organizers chose to formulate the discussions in a way that does not align with official EU positions." France remained the only major sponsor.

---

**Why the EU Avoids Calling the US a FIMI Actor**

- **Alliance and trade interests.** The US is a NATO ally and the largest trading partner; officially designating it an "enemy" would have major diplomatic and economic consequences.

- **Legal and political sensitivity.** The FIMI concept was created by the EU primarily to describe Russia and China; applying it to an ally would mean that the EU would have to apply sanctions or other "deterrence" measures against the US.

- **US pressure.** The Vilnius incident showed that Washington actively opposes any attempt to classify it among FIMI actors.

---

**Conclusion**

**Legally and officially – the US is not designated as a FIMI threat to Europe.** In EU documents, Russia and China remain the primary threats, and the US remains a partner (albeit an increasingly unreliable one).

**However, practically and analytically – this is an open, unresolved, and increasingly sharp issue.** A significant portion of the anti-disinformation community, researchers, and some member states (e.g., France) believe that actions supported by the US government – political interference in elections, funding of MAGA groups, the use of platforms for political purposes – essentially meet the definition of FIMI. EU institutions are deliberately avoiding this designation for now, but the Vilnius conference scandal showed that this "uncomfortable topic" is no longer marginal – it has become a central issue in transatlantic relations.

**Original text**

**Trumpa atsakymas**

**Oficialiai – ne.** ES institucijų dokumentuose Jungtinės Valstijos nėra įvardijamos kaip FIMI (užsienio informacijos manipuliavimo ir kišimosi) grėsmė. Oficialiuose ES vertinimuose pagrindiniais FIMI šaltiniais įvardijami **Rusija ir Kinija** (o hibridinių grėsmių kontekste – dar Baltarusija, Iranas, Šiaurės Korėja). Tačiau **2025–2026 m. šis klausimas tapo vienu jautriausių ir atviriausiai diskutuojamų transatlantinių ginčų**, ir dalis tyrėjų, pilietinės visuomenės bei kai kurių valstybių narių vis dažniau teigia, kad JAV vyriausybės veiksmai iš esmės atitinka FIMI apibrėžimą.

---

**Ką sako oficialūs ES dokumentai**

- **EEAS 4-asis FIMI grėsmių reportas (2026 m. kovas):** iš visų dokumentuotų incidentų **35 % priskirta Rusijai (29 %) ir Kinijai (6 %)**. Rusija įvardijama kaip pagrindinė grėsmė, o JAV neminima kaip FIMI veikėja.

- **Europos Parlamento specialiojo komiteto „Europos demokratijos skydas“ (EUDS) ataskaita (priimta 2026 m. birželį, EP patvirtinta rugsėjį):** „Rusija – pagrindinė išorinė hibridinė grėsmė“, taip pat nurodomos Baltarusija, Kinija, Iranas, Šiaurės Korėja. JAV kaip FIMI šaltinis nenurodoma – nors ataskaitoje raginama mažinti priklausomybę nuo **JAV technologijų kompanijų** kritinėse srityse.

- **ES vyriausiosios įgaliotinės Kaja Kallas kalba 2026 m. FIMI konferencijoje:** „Mes daugiausia žiūrime į Rusijos ir Kinijos remiamą kišimąsi, nes tai – mūsų pačių iššūkiai. Bet tas pats metodas gali būti taikomas grėsmėms iš bet kurios šalies.“ Ji taip pat atkreipė dėmesį į „didžiulę spragą, kurią paliko Jungtinės Valstijos“ – JAV Valstybės departamentas sustabdė didžiąją dalį kovos su dezinformacija darbo, o tai sulėtino G7 ir NATO pastangas.

Taigi ES oficialiai JAV traktuoja **ne kaip grėsmę, o kaip partnerę** (bent jau istoriškai – ES ir JAV 2023–2024 m. Prekybos ir technologijų taryboje net sukūrė bendrą FIMI metodiką ir keitėsi žvalgybine informacija).

---

**Kodėl vis dėlto kyla klausimas „ar JAV – FIMI grėsmė?“**

Nuo 2025 m. susikaupė nemažai faktų, kuriuos tyrėjai laiko atitinkančiais FIMI apibrėžimą:

1. **JAV vyriausybės finansavimas MAGA ideologijai artimoms grupėms Europoje.** Valstybės departamento programa siūlo 1–3 mln. USD dotacijas organizacijoms, siekiančioms „nacionalinio suvereniteto, migracijos, cenzūros ir lawfare“ tikslų. Vokietijos kancleris **Friedrichas Merzas 2026 m. liepą viešai perspėjo JAV nesikišti** į Vokietijos rinkimus. Prancūzų grupės iš programos buvo išbrauktos dėl baimės, kad tai bus pavadinta kišimusi prieš 2027 m. prezidento rinkimus.

1. **Tiesioginis politinis kišimasis į rinkimus.** Viceprezidentas **J. D. Vance'as 2026 m. balandį vyko į Budapeštą paremti Viktoro Orbáno** prieš rinkimus, o valstybės sekretorius Marco Rubio anksčiau viešai jį palaikė. JAV 2025 m. Nacionalinė saugumo strategija teigia, kad Europai gresia „civilizacinė išnykimas“.

1. **Platformų dimensija.** ES 2025 m. gruodį **skyrė „X“ 120 mln. eurų baudą** pagal Skaitmeninių paslaugų aktą (DSA) dėl „klaidinančio“ mėlynojo varnelės dizaino, reklamos skaidrumo ir tyrėjų prieigos. JAV Teisingumo departamentas **įsikišo į teisminį procesą** palaikydamas Muską ir kaltindamas ES „reguliavimo peržengimu“. Muskas viešai rėmė Vokietijos AfD ir kitas dešiniąsias jėgas prieš rinkimus.

1. **Diplomatinis spaudimas dėl paties termino.** 2026 m. spalį Vilniuje vykusioje **#Disinfo2026 konferencijoje** buvo numatyta panelė **„JAV kaip FIMI grėsmė: pripažinti, prisitaikyti, atsispirti“**. JAV Valstybės departamentas darė spaudimą Lietuvai, Kanadai ir Prancūzijai atsisakyti paramos renginiui. Lietuvos URM ir „Global Affairs Canada“ atsitraukė, o **EEAS pašalino savo logotipą**, teigdamas, kad „organizatoriai pasirinko diskusijas formuluoti taip, kad jos neatitinka oficialių ES pozicijų“. Prancūzija liko vienintelė pagrindinė rėmėja.

---

**Kodėl ES vengia JAV vadinti FIMI veikėja**

- **Aljanso ir prekybos interesai.** JAV – NATO sąjungininkė ir didžiausia prekybos partnerė; oficialus „priešo“ įvardijimas turėtų didelių diplomatinių ir ekonominių pasekmių.

- **Teisinis ir politinis jautrumas.** FIMI sąvoka ES sukurta pirmiausia Rusijai ir Kinijai apibūdinti; jos taikymas sąjungininkei reikštų, kad ES turėtų taikyti sankcijas ar kitas „atgrasymo“ priemones prieš JAV.

- **JAV spaudimas.** Vilniaus incidentas parodė, kad Vašingtonas aktyviai priešinasi bet kokiam bandymui jį priskirti prie FIMI veikėjų.

---

**Išvada**

**Teisiškai ir oficialiai – JAV nėra įvardijama kaip FIMI grėsmė Europai.** ES dokumentuose pagrindinėmis grėsmėmis lieka Rusija ir Kinija, o JAV – partnerė (nors ir vis labiau nepatikima).

**Tačiau praktiškai ir analitiškai – tai atviras, neišspręstas ir vis aštresnis klausimas.** Nemaža dalis kovos su dezinformacija bendruomenės, tyrėjų ir kai kurių valstybių narių (pvz., Prancūzijos) mano, kad JAV vyriausybės remiami veiksmai – politinis kišimasis į rinkimus, MAGA grupių finansavimas, platformų naudojimas politiniams tikslams – iš esmės atitinka FIMI apibrėžimą. ES institucijos kol kas sąmoningai vengia šio įvardijimo, tačiau Vilniaus konferencijos skandalas parodė, kad ši „nepatogi tema“ nebėra marginalinė – ji tapo centriniu transatlantinių santykių klausimu.

#### Russian — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

**Short Answer**

**Formally — no. Politically — increasingly yes, but with caveats.**

The EU's official framework for countering FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) still describes the threat as originating from **Russia and China**, while the United States figures as a partner rather than a threat actor. However, since 2025, actions by the US administration, Elon Musk, and US platforms have increasingly been qualified in European political and expert discourse as interference in Europe's internal affairs — with several EU officials effectively hinting at this without naming the US directly.

---

**1. Official EU Position: The US is Not a FIMI Actor**

- **EEAS Reports on FIMI Threats.** The 3rd report (March 2025, data for 2024) and the 4th report (data for 2025) describe the threat as originating from "authoritarian regimes, primarily Russia and China." In the 4th report: 540 incidents, 35% attributed — Russia (29%) and China (6%). The US is not on the list of actors. ([EEAS, 3rd Report](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2025/EEAS-3nd-ThreatReport-March-2025-05-Digital-HD.pdf), [EEAS, 4th Report](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2026/documents/EEAS%204th%20Threat%20Report_web.pdf))

- **The US as a Partner.** The EU and the US jointly developed a common methodology for FIMI analysis (DISARM/STIX/OpenCTI) within the framework of the Trade and Technology Council, and issued a joint statement (April 2024) on FIMI risks. ([EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2023/Annex%203%20-%20FIMI_29%20May.docx.pdf))

- **Kaja Kallas's Formulation (2026).** "We are looking primarily at Russia- and China-backed interference, because these are our own challenges. But the same approach can be applied to threats originating from any country." This is more of a hint than an accusation. ([EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en))

- In the 4th report, the US appears not as an actor, but as a **topic** exploited by Russian FIMI campaigns ("transatlantic relations" being one of the exploited narratives).

**2. What Changed in 2025: The "Grey Zone"**

A number of US actions began to be described by European politicians and analysts as interference:

| Event | Essence |
|---|---|
| **Vance's Speech in Munich (Feb. 2025)** | Declared the "threat from within" to be the main threat to Europe, called "misinformation/disinformation" "Soviet in spirit words," stated that "expressing an opinion is not election interference, even if you are very influential," and criticized the annulment of elections in Romania and the "firewall" around the AfD. ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/vance-uses-munich-speech-criticize-europe-censoring-free-speech-2025-02-14/)) |
| **Musk and X** | Open support for the AfD, attacks on European leaders, algorithmic amplification; debates in the European Parliament (Jan. 21, 2025); investigation under the DSA; **€120 million fine (Dec. 5, 2025)** — the first ruling on DSA non-compliance. ([EC](https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/commission-fines-x-eu120-million-under-digital-services-act)) |
| **US Visa Sanctions Against Europeans (Dec. 2025)** | Entry bans for former EU Commissioner Thierry Breton and four activists/NGOs for "censoring American views"; previously — a visa restriction policy (May 2025). ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/legal/government/us-targets-former-eu-commissioner-activists-with-visa-bans-over-alleged-2025-12-23/), [AP](https://apnews.com/article/europe-breton-tech-visa-ban-censorship-rubio-8608c3781b67958abb92e4781d73aed9)) |
| **Termination of Cooperation on Disinformation** | The US notified ~22 European and African countries of the termination of memoranda on countering disinformation (Sept. 2025); previously, the GEC, R/FIMI, the FBI's FITF, CISA election monitoring, and FMIC were closed. ([EUISS](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf)) |
| **US National Security Strategy (Dec. 2025)** | The thesis of the "civilizational erasure" of Europe and the goal to "cultivate resistance to Europe’s current trajectory within European nations." Reaction: Costa — "allies do not threaten interference in the internal political decisions of allies"; von der Leyen — "no one else should interfere." ([AP](https://apnews.com/article/europe-us-security-strategy-russia-interference-dd672c0d0b3f31378c39381ef8ea8dea), [Politico](https://www.politico.eu/article/european-democracy-ursula-von-der-leyen-donald-trump/)) |

Commissioner for Democracy Michael McGrath’s formulation is telling: Russia is "not the only actor" that has an "interest" in influencing elections; Politico directly concludes: "Trump's America must now be counted among them." ([Politico](https://www.politico.eu/article/donald-trump-european-elections/))

**3. Does This Fit the Definition of FIMI?**

The EU definition requires: a **foreign state actor** (or its proxy), **intentional manipulative activity**, with the goal to **mislead, harm, and destabilize**.

**Arguments "for":** - US officials openly campaign for specific European parties and against incumbent governments. - The National Security Strategy explicitly formulates the goal of influencing Europe's internal political trajectory — this is intentional impact. - Visa sanctions against European regulators — coercive pressure on sovereign EU decisions. - Algorithmic amplification of content by a platform owner (X) — manipulation of the information environment.

**Arguments "against":** - Actions are **open**, not covert: classical FIMI is built on clandestine networks, fake media, "doppelgangers" — the US has not been found to have such an infrastructural arsenal in Europe. - The US is a NATO ally, not an "authoritarian regime"; the FIMI concept in the EU was originally designed with Russia and China in mind. - Much of the US rhetoric is presented as a defense of freedom of speech rather than disinformation. - The EU institutionally avoids naming the US so as not to destroy the alliance framework.

It is more accurate to say that this is about **"foreign interference" in a broad sense** and the **problem of platform governance**, rather than classical FIMI in a narrow sense. Although individual elements (algorithmic boosting, unlabeled state-linked content, AI generation) may fall under FIMI criteria.

**4. How the EU is Responding**

- **European Democracy Shield** (Nov. 12, 2025) and the **European Centre for Democratic Resilience** — directly aimed at FIMI and disinformation, but the wording is neutral ("threats from any country"). ([EC](https://commission.europa.eu/news-and-media/news/stronger-measures-protect-our-democracy-and-civil-society-2025-11-12_en))

- **DSA enforcement** against American platforms (X, Meta, TikTok).

- **Fact-checkers network**, Rapid Alert System, expansion of the sanctions regime for hybrid actions.

- At the same time, EU analytical documents explicitly acknowledge a "strategic vulnerability": the EU information ecosystem relies on American and Chinese platforms, and the "increasingly confrontational rhetoric of the US administration" creates additional pressure. ([EPRS](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/BRIE/2025/775835/EPRS_BRI(2025)775835_EN.pdf))

---

**Conclusion**

- **Legally/Officially:** The US is **not** designated as a FIMI threat actor. EU institutions continue to describe FIMI as a Russian-Chinese threat, and the US as a partner (albeit an increasingly unreliable one).

- **Politically and de facto:** since 2025, a significant part of the European establishment and expert community views the actions of the US administration, Musk, and US platforms as a form of **foreign interference** in European politics. EU officials (McGrath, Kallas, Costa, von der Leyen) phrase this cautiously, but recognizably.

- **Correct Formulation:** Today, the US is **not a recognized FIMI actor, but a recognized source of "foreign interference" and a growing risk factor** for Europe's information sovereignty. This is a controversial and politically charged issue, and the answer depends on whether we apply a narrow definition of FIMI (covert manipulative networks) or a broad one (any external impact on democratic processes).

**Original text**

**Короткий ответ**

**Формально — нет. Политически — всё больше да, но с оговорками.**

Официальная архитектура ЕС по противодействию FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) по-прежнему описывает угрозу как исходящую от **России и Китая**, а США фигурируют как партнёр, а не как субъект угрозы. Однако с 2025 года в европейском политическом и экспертном дискурсе действия американской администрации, Илона Маска и американских платформ всё чаще квалифицируются как вмешательство во внутренние дела Европы — и некоторые официальные лица ЕС фактически намекают на это, не называя США прямо.

---

**1. Официальная позиция ЕС: США — не субъект FIMI**

- **Доклады EEAS о угрозах FIMI.** 3-й доклад (март 2025, данные за 2024) и 4-й доклад (данные за 2025) описывают угрозу как исходящую от «авторитарных режимов, прежде всего России и Китая». В 4-м докладе: 540 инцидентов, 35% атрибутированы — Россия (29%) и Китай (6%). США в списке акторов нет. ([EEAS, 3-й доклад](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2025/EEAS-3nd-ThreatReport-March-2025-05-Digital-HD.pdf), [EEAS, 4-й доклад](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2026/documents/EEAS%204th%20Threat%20Report_web.pdf))

- **США как партнёр.** ЕС и США совместно разработали общую методологию анализа FIMI (DISARM/STIX/OpenCTI) в рамках Совета по торговле и технологиям, выпустили совместное заявление (апрель 2024) о рисках FIMI. ([EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2023/Annex%203%20-%20FIMI_29%20May.docx.pdf))

- **Формулировка Каи Каллас (2026).** «Мы смотрим преимущественно на поддержанное Россией и Китаем вмешательство, потому что это наши собственные вызовы. Но тот же подход может быть применён к угрозам, исходящим из любой страны». Это скорее намёк, чем обвинение. ([EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en))

- В 4-м докладе США фигурируют не как актор, а как **тема**, которую эксплуатируют российские FIMI-кампании («трансатлантические отношения» — один из эксплуатируемых сюжетов).

**2. Что изменилось в 2025 году: «серая зона»**

Ряд действий США европейские политики и аналитики стали описывать как вмешательство:

| Событие | Суть |
|---|---|
| **Речь Вэнса в Мюнхене (фев. 2025)** | Объявил главной угрозой Европе «угрозу изнутри», назвал «misinformation/disinformation» «советскими по духу словами», заявил, что «высказывать мнение — это не вмешательство в выборы, даже если ты очень влиятелен», раскритиковал аннулирование выборов в Румынии и «файрвол» вокруг AfD. ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/vance-uses-munich-speech-criticize-europe-censoring-free-speech-2025-02-14/)) |
| **Маск и X** | Открытая поддержка AfD, атаки на европейских лидеров, алгоритмическое усиление; дебаты в Европарламенте (21 янв. 2025); расследование по DSA; **штраф €120 млн (5 дек. 2025)** — первое решение о несоответствии DSA. ([ЕК](https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/commission-fines-x-eu120-million-under-digital-services-act)) |
| **Визовые санкции США против европейцев (дек. 2025)** | Запрет на въезд для экс-комиссара ЕС Тьерри Бретона и четырёх активистов/НКО за «цензуру американских взглядов»; ранее — политика визовых ограничений (май 2025). ([Reuters](https://www.reuters.com/legal/government/us-targets-former-eu-commissioner-activists-with-visa-bans-over-alleged-2025-12-23/), [AP](https://apnews.com/article/europe-breton-tech-visa-ban-censorship-rubio-8608c3781b67958abb92e4781d73aed9)) |
| **Разрыв сотрудничества по дезинформации** | США уведомили ~22 страны Европы и Африки о расторжении меморандумов о противодействии дезинформации (сент. 2025); ранее закрыты GEC, R/FIMI, FITF в ФБР, мониторинг выборов в CISA, FMIC. ([EUISS](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf)) |
| **Стратегия нацбезопасности США (дек. 2025)** | Тезис о «цивилизационном стирании» Европы и о цели «культивировать сопротивление нынешней траектории Европы внутри европейских наций». Реакция: Коста — «союзники не угрожают вмешательством во внутренние политические решения союзников»; фон дер Ляйен — «никто другой не должен вмешиваться». ([AP](https://apnews.com/article/europe-us-security-strategy-russia-interference-dd672c0d0b3f31378c39381ef8ea8dea), [Politico](https://www.politico.eu/article/european-democracy-ursula-von-der-leyen-donald-trump/)) |

Показательна формулировка комиссара по демократии Майкла Макграта: Россия — «не единственный актор», у которого есть «заинтересованность» влиять на выборы; Politico прямо заключает: «Америка Трампа теперь должна считаться среди них». ([Politico](https://www.politico.eu/article/donald-trump-european-elections/))

**3. Подходит ли это под определение FIMI?**

Определение ЕС требует: **иностранный государственный актор** (или его прокси), **намеренная манипулятивная деятельность**, цель — **ввести в заблуждение, нанести вред, дестабилизировать**.

**Аргументы «за»:** - Американские официальные лица открыто агитируют за конкретные европейские партии и против действующих правительств. - Стратегия нацбезопасности прямо формулирует цель влиять на внутриполитическую траекторию Европы — это намеренное воздействие. - Визовые санкции против европейских регуляторов — принудительное давление на суверенные решения ЕС. - Алгоритмическое усиление контента владельцем платформы (X) — манипуляция информационной средой.

**Аргументы «против»:** - Действия **открытые**, а не скрытые: классическая FIMI строится на тайных сетях, фейковых СМИ, «доппельгангерах» — у США такого инфраструктурного арсенала в Европе не выявлено. - США — союзник по НАТО, а не «авторитарный режим»; концепция FIMI в ЕС изначально проектировалась под Россию и Китай. - Значительная часть риторики США подаётся как защита свободы слова, а не как дезинформация. - ЕС институционально избегает называть США, чтобы не разрушить союзнические рамки.

Точнее говорить, что речь идёт о **«иностранном вмешательстве» (foreign interference) в широком смысле** и о **проблеме управления платформами**, а не о классической FIMI в узком смысле. Хотя отдельные элементы (алгоритмическая накрутка, неразмеченный государственно-связанный контент, ИИ-генерация) могут подпадать под FIMI-критерии.

**4. Как ЕС реагирует**

- **European Democracy Shield** (12 нояб. 2025) и **Европейский центр демократической устойчивости** — прямо нацелены на FIMI и дезинформацию, но формулировки нейтральны («угрозы из любой страны»). ([ЕК](https://commission.europa.eu/news-and-media/news/stronger-measures-protect-our-democracy-and-civil-society-2025-11-12_en))

- **Правоприменение DSA** против американских платформ (X, Meta, TikTok).

- **Сеть фактчекеров**, Rapid Alert System, расширение санкционного режима за гибридные действия.

- При этом в аналитических документах ЕС прямо признаётся «стратегическая уязвимость»: информационная экосистема ЕС опирается на американские и китайские платформы, а «всё более конфронтационная риторика американской администрации» создаёт дополнительное давление. ([EPRS](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/BRIE/2025/775835/EPRS_BRI(2025)775835_EN.pdf))

---

**Итог**

- **Юридически/официально:** США **не** обозначены как субъект FIMI-угрозы. Институты ЕС продолжают описывать FIMI как российско-китайскую угрозу, а США — как партнёра (пусть и всё менее надёжного).

- **Политически и де-факто:** с 2025 года значительная часть европейского истеблишмента и экспертного сообщества рассматривает действия американской администрации, Маска и американских платформ как форму **иностранного вмешательства** в европейскую политику. Официальные лица ЕС (Макграт, Каллас, Коста, фон дер Ляйен) формулируют это осторожно, но узнаваемо.

- **Корректная формулировка:** США сегодня — это **не признанный FIMI-актор, но признаваемый источник «иностранного вмешательства» и растущий фактор риска** для информационного суверенитета Европы. Это спорный и политически заряженный вопрос, и ответ зависит от того, применяем ли мы узкое определение FIMI (скрытые манипулятивные сети) или широкое (любое внешнее воздействие на демократические процессы).

**English translation**

Officially and diplomatically, **no—the European Union does not classify the United States as a Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference (FIMI) threat.** However, whether American actors and information flows pose a threat to European democratic discourse has become a subject of growing debate among civil society researchers, digital rights advocates, and policymakers.

---

**1. The Official Institutional Stance (EU & Allies)** * **Designated Adversaries:** The European External Action Service (EEAS), which developed the FIMI framework, formally focuses its monitoring and attribution on hostile foreign state actors—primarily **Russia** and **China**. * **The U.S. as a Partner:** Officially, the EU views the United States as a democratic ally and key partner in countering FIMI. The two coordinate closely through multilateral frameworks (such as the G7 Rapid Response Mechanism and NATO) and bilateral initiatives (such as the EU–U.S. Trade and Technology Council). * **Rejection of the Label:** The EU has actively distanced itself from attempts to label the U.S. a FIMI actor. For instance, when the independent NGO EU DisinfoLab hosted a conference panel titled *"USA as a FIMI threat: adapt, acknowledge, push back,"* the EEAS and European partner governments removed their official logos from the event. EU spokespersons reaffirmed that framing the U.S. as a FIMI threat "does not align with the official positions held by the EU."

---

**2. Why the Issue is Debated in Research and Civil Society** While European governments reject treating the U.S. as a hostile foreign adversary, independent researchers, European media, and digital policy analysts frequently debate non-state and political information risks originating from the U.S.:

- **Social Media Infrastructure and Platform Governance:** Most major digital platforms used by Europeans (such as Meta, X, and YouTube) are U.S.-based. Algorithmic changes, shifts in content moderation policies, and disputes over compliance with the EU's Digital Services Act (DSA) have led European regulators to view U.S. platform policies as a systemic vulnerability for information integrity.

- **The "Export" of Polarization and Disinformation:** Conspiracy theories (such as QAnon-style narratives), anti-climate action campaigns, and culture-war rhetoric originating in the U.S. ecosystem routinely spill over into European media, fueling domestic polarization.

- **Transnational Political Influence and Funding:** Concerns have been raised by European investigative journalists and civil society organizations regarding U.S. conservative foundations, political action networks, and funding streams supporting far-right or eurosceptic movements across Europe.

- **Shifting U.S. Foreign Policy Postures:** Fluctuations in U.S. administrations have raised concerns in Brussels regarding transatlantic alignment on counter-disinformation efforts, with debates over free speech absolutism versus European regulatory approaches to digital safety.

---

**Summary** Under the strict EU definition of FIMI—which emphasizes coordinated, deceptive, and hostile operations by foreign adversaries to undermine democratic institutions—**the United States is an ally, not a threat actor.** Nevertheless, the immense cultural, technical, and political influence of the U.S. information ecosystem remains a significant, often contentious vulnerability that European regulators and civil society closely monitor.

**Original text**

Officially and diplomatically, **no—the European Union does not classify the United States as a Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference (FIMI) threat.** However, whether American actors and information flows pose a threat to European democratic discourse has become a subject of growing debate among civil society researchers, digital rights advocates, and policymakers.

---

**1. The Official Institutional Stance (EU & Allies)** * **Designated Adversaries:** The European External Action Service (EEAS), which developed the FIMI framework, formally focuses its monitoring and attribution on hostile foreign state actors—primarily **Russia** and **China**. * **The U.S. as a Partner:** Officially, the EU views the United States as a democratic ally and key partner in countering FIMI. The two coordinate closely through multilateral frameworks (such as the G7 Rapid Response Mechanism and NATO) and bilateral initiatives (such as the EU–U.S. Trade and Technology Council). * **Rejection of the Label:** The EU has actively distanced itself from attempts to label the U.S. a FIMI actor. For instance, when the independent NGO EU DisinfoLab hosted a conference panel titled *"USA as a FIMI threat: adapt, acknowledge, push back,"* the EEAS and European partner governments removed their official logos from the event. EU spokespersons reaffirmed that framing the U.S. as a FIMI threat "does not align with the official positions held by the EU."

---

**2. Why the Issue is Debated in Research and Civil Society** While European governments reject treating the U.S. as a hostile foreign adversary, independent researchers, European media, and digital policy analysts frequently debate non-state and political information risks originating from the U.S.:

- **Social Media Infrastructure and Platform Governance:** Most major digital platforms used by Europeans (such as Meta, X, and YouTube) are U.S.-based. Algorithmic changes, shifts in content moderation policies, and disputes over compliance with the EU's Digital Services Act (DSA) have led European regulators to view U.S. platform policies as a systemic vulnerability for information integrity.

- **The "Export" of Polarization and Disinformation:** Conspiracy theories (such as QAnon-style narratives), anti-climate action campaigns, and culture-war rhetoric originating in the U.S. ecosystem routinely spill over into European media, fueling domestic polarization.

- **Transnational Political Influence and Funding:** Concerns have been raised by European investigative journalists and civil society organizations regarding U.S. conservative foundations, political action networks, and funding streams supporting far-right or eurosceptic movements across Europe.

- **Shifting U.S. Foreign Policy Postures:** Fluctuations in U.S. administrations have raised concerns in Brussels regarding transatlantic alignment on counter-disinformation efforts, with debates over free speech absolutism versus European regulatory approaches to digital safety.
