#### French — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

Based on research conducted on official European reports, recent threat assessments, and the current geopolitical context, here is a nuanced analysis of this complex question:

**Summary Answer**

**Officially, the United States is not classified as a FIMI threat by European institutions**—they remain a strategic partner in the fight against information manipulation. However, **the situation is evolving rapidly in 2025**, with the emergence of growing concerns regarding American behaviors that could amount to interference, particularly via private actors linked to the Trump administration.

---

**1. The Official European Framework: The United States as a Partner, Not a Threat**

The most recent reports from the European External Action Service (EEAS) are unambiguous:

**The 3rd EEAS Report on FIMI Threats (March 2025)** explicitly identifies **Russia and China** as the main actors of information manipulation targeting the EU. The analysis of 505 FIMI incidents in 2024 reveals a massive infrastructure attributed to these two authoritarian regimes, accounting for 20% and 3.5% of the detected FIMI architecture, respectively.

**The 2024 EEAS Report** highlights close cooperation with the United States: *"Cooperation with the United Kingdom and Canada on FIMI-related issues remained strong,"* and mentions the EU-US joint statement during the EU-US Trade and Technology Council (TTC) in April 2024, where both parties agreed to use the same methodology (DISARM, STIX 2, OpenCTI) to detect and analyze FIMI threats.

**The European Parliament** (Report A10-0250/2025 on EU-US political relations) states that *"the Union, its Member States and the United States face similar and persistent hybrid threats, notably foreign information manipulation and interference activities"*—placing the United States on the side of potential victims, not threats.

---

**2. The Concerning Evolution of 2025: Towards Questioning the Partnership**

Several recent developments suggest a degradation of transatlantic trust in the informational domain:

**a) The American Dismantling of Anti-Disinformation Structures** The Trump administration has dissolved the State Department's **Global Engagement Center (GEC)**, the agency responsible for combating foreign disinformation. The United States has also **unilaterally terminated** cooperation agreements signed in 2024 with Europe to jointly combat Russian, Chinese, and Iranian disinformation. James Rubin, former director of the GEC, described this decision as *"a unilateral act of disarmament"* in the information war.

**b) An Increasingly Hostile Rhetoric** According to the EU Institute for Security Studies (ISS), *"what unfolded in 2025 goes several steps further"* in the transatlantic rupture: - Trump described the EU as a *"globalist"* entity aiming to *"rip off"* the United States - He refused to rule out the use of force to annex Greenland (territory of an EU Member State and NATO ally) - Vice President **JD Vance** declared at the Munich Conference that attempts to limit disinformation represented a greater threat to Europe than Russia and China - The State Department published a memorandum accusing Europe of waging an *"aggressive campaign against Western civilization itself"*

**c) Interference via Private Actors: The Case of Elon Musk** Although Musk is not a state actor, his close ties to the Trump administration and his role within it (Department of Government Efficiency - DOGE) are raising European concerns:

- Members of the European Parliament (notably Damian Boeselager) have asked the European Commission to investigate Musk's **"interference"** in European elections

- An analysis by franceinfo/RTBF/France 24 (April 2025) shows that Musk used X to **systematically amplify the far right in Europe**, notably the AfD in Germany (nearly 200 tweets about Germany, 7 out of 10 concerning immigration or the AfD)

- French Foreign Minister Jean-Noël Barrot asked the EU to intervene with *"the utmost firmness,"* even raising the possibility of banning X in Europe

- The European Commission has been investigating X since 2023 under the Digital Services Act (DSA)

---

**3. European Fears Regarding Dependence on American Intelligence**

Several recent articles (Foreign Policy, Washington Post, 2025) reveal that European intelligence services are concerned about: - The **reliability of intelligence sharing** with the United States under Trump - The risk that sensitive information shared with Washington could end up in the hands of Moscow or Trump loyalists - Trump's willingness to exclude Canada from the Five Eyes group - The cessation of American cyberattacks against Russia (ordered by Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth), creating space for Russian FIMI operations

---

**4. Historical Context: The United States Has Conducted Influence Operations in Europe**

It is important to note that the United States has historically conducted influence operations in Europe, particularly during the Cold War (CIA, funding of political parties, media operations). However, these activities took place within a framework of common defense against the USSR and were not considered FIMI threats in the modern sense of the term.

---

**Conclusion**

| Dimension | Assessment |
|---|---|
| **Official Status** | Strategic partner, not classified as a FIMI threat |
| **EU Legal Framework** | The United States enjoys allied status in FIMI cooperation mechanisms |
| **2024-2025 Evolution** | Rapid degradation of trust; concerning American behaviors |
| **Potential Threat** | Emerging, primarily via:<br>• The dismantling of American anti-disinformation structures<br>• Interference by private actors linked to the administration (Musk)<br>• Hostile rhetoric and geopolitical threats<br>• The calling into question of intelligence sharing |
| **Global Assessment** | **Not a systemic FIMI threat at this stage**, but a **growing risk** requiring increased vigilance and Europe's autonomous resilience strategy |

ISS analysts now speak of a **"low trust"** environment in transatlantic relations, where Europe must prepare to **take over** the fight against disinformation, potentially including against allied actors who have become hostile.

---

**Main sources consulted:** - EEAS, *3rd Report on Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference (FIMI) Threats*, March 2025 - EEAS, *2024 Report on EEAS Activities to Counter FIMI*, 2025 - European Parliament, *Report on political relations between the EU and the United States* (A10-0250/2025) - ISS, *Low trust: navigating transatlantic relations under Trump 2.0*, 2025 - EU DisinfoLab, *Documenting the setbacks: The new environment for counter-disinformation in Europe*, 2025 - International press (The Guardian, Politico, Franceinfo, CNN, Foreign Policy, Washington Post), January–June 2025

**Original text**

D'après les recherches effectuées sur les rapports officiels européens, les évaluations de menaces récentes et le contexte géopolitique actuel, voici une analyse nuancée de cette question complexe :

**Réponse synthétique**

**Officiellement, les États-Unis ne sont pas classés comme une menace FIMI par les institutions européennes** — ils demeurent un partenaire stratégique dans la lutte contre la manipulation de l'information. Cependant, **la situation évolue rapidement en 2025**, avec l'émergence de préoccupations croissantes concernant des comportements américains qui pourraient s'apparenter à de l'ingérence, notamment via des acteurs privés liés à l'administration Trump.

---

**1. Le cadre officiel européen : les États-Unis comme partenaire, non comme menace**

Les rapports les plus récents du Service européen pour l'action extérieure (EEAS) sont sans ambiguïté :

**Le 3e rapport EEAS sur les menaces FIMI (mars 2025)** identifie explicitement **la Russie et la Chine** comme les principaux acteurs de manipulation de l'information ciblant l'UE. L'analyse de 505 incidents FIMI en 2024 révèle une infrastructure massive attribuée à ces deux régimes autoritaires, représentant respectivement 20% et 3,5% de l'architecture FIMI détectée.

**Le rapport 2024 de l'EEAS** souligne la coopération étroite avec les États-Unis : *"Cooperation with the United Kingdom and Canada on FIMI-related issues remained strong"* et mentionne la déclaration conjointe UE-États-Unis lors du Conseil UE-US du commerce et de la technologie (TTC) d'avril 2024, où les deux parties ont convenu d'utiliser la même méthodologie (DISARM, STIX 2, OpenCTI) pour détecter et analyser les menaces FIMI.

**Le Parlement européen** (rapport A10-0250/2025 sur les relations politiques UE-États-Unis) affirme que *"l'Union, ses États membres et les États-Unis sont confrontés à des menaces hybrides similaires et persistantes, notamment des activités de manipulation de l'information et d'ingérence menées depuis l'étranger"* — plaçant les États-Unis du côté des victimes potentielles, non des menaces.

---

**2. L'évolution préoccupante de 2025 : vers une remise en question du partenariat**

Plusieurs développements récents suggèrent une dégradation de la confiance transatlantique dans le domaine informationnel :

**a) Le démantèlement américain des structures anti-désinformation** L'administration Trump a dissous le **Global Engagement Center (GEC)** du Département d'État, l'agence chargée de combattre la désinformation étrangère. Les États-Unis ont également **résilié unilatéralement** les accords de coopération signés en 2024 avec l'Europe pour lutter conjointement contre la désinformation russe, chinoise et iranienne. James Rubin, ancien directeur du GEC, a qualifié cette décision d'*"acte unilatéral de désarmement"* dans la guerre de l'information.

**b) Une rhétorique de plus en plus hostile** Selon l'Institut d'études de sécurité de l'UE (ISS), *"ce qui s'est déroulé en 2025 va plusieurs étapes plus loin"* dans la rupture transatlantique : - Trump a qualifié l'UE d'entité *"globaliste"* visant à *"arnaquer"* les États-Unis - Il a refusé d'exclure l'usage de la force pour annexer le Groenland (territoire d'un État membre de l'UE et allié de l'OTAN) - **JD Vance**, vice-président, a déclaré à la Conférence de Munich que les tentatives de limiter la désinformation représentaient une menace plus grande pour l'Europe que la Russie et la Chine - Le Département d'État a publié un mémorandum accusant l'Europe de mener une *"campagne agressive contre la civilisation occidentale elle-même"*

**c) L'ingérence via des acteurs privés : le cas Elon Musk** Bien que Musk ne soit pas un acteur étatique, ses liens étroits avec l'administration Trump et son rôle au sein de celle-ci (Département de l'Efficacité Gouvernementale - DOGE) suscitent des inquiétudes européennes :

- Des eurodéputés (notamment Damian Boeselager) ont demandé à la Commission européenne d'enquêter sur les **"ingérences"** de Musk dans les élections européennes

- Une analyse de franceinfo/RTBF/France 24 (avril 2025) montre que Musk a utilisé X pour **amplifier systématiquement l'extrême droite en Europe**, notamment l'AfD en Allemagne (près de 200 tweets sur l'Allemagne, 7 sur 10 concernant l'immigration ou l'AfD)

- Le ministre français des Affaires étrangères Jean-Noël Barrot a demandé à l'UE d'intervenir avec *"la plus grande fermeté"*, évoquant même la possibilité d'interdire X en Europe

- La Commission européenne enquête depuis 2023 sur X dans le cadre du Digital Services Act (DSA)

---

**3. Les craintes européennes concernant la dépendance au renseignement américain**

Plusieurs articles récents (Foreign Policy, Washington Post, 2025) révèlent que les services de renseignement européens s'inquiètent de : - La **fiabilité du partage de renseignement** avec les États-Unis sous Trump - Le risque que des informations sensibles partagées avec Washington puissent finir entre les mains de Moscou ou d'adeptes de Trump - La volonté de Trump d'exclure le Canada du groupe Five Eyes - L'arrêt des cyberattaques américaines contre la Russie (ordonné par le secrétaire à la Défense Pete Hegseth), créant un espace pour les opérations FIMI russes

---

**4. Contexte historique : les États-Unis ont mené des opérations d'influence en Europe**

Il est important de noter que les États-Unis ont historiquement mené des opérations d'influence en Europe, notamment pendant la Guerre froide (CIA, financement de partis politiques, opérations médiatiques). Cependant, ces activités s'inscrivaient dans un cadre de défense commune contre l'URSS et n'étaient pas considérées comme des menaces FIMI au sens actuel du terme.

---

**Conclusion**

| Dimension | Évaluation |
|---|---|
| **Statut officiel** | Partenaire stratégique, non classé comme menace FIMI |
| **Cadre juridique UE** | Les États-Unis bénéficient d'un statut d'allié dans les mécanismes de coopération FIMI |
| **Évolution 2024-2025** | Dégradation rapide de la confiance ; comportements américains préoccupants |
| **Menace potentielle** | Émergente, principalement via :<br>• Le démantèlement des structures anti-désinformation américaines<br>• L'ingérence d'acteurs privés liés à l'administration (Musk)<br>• La rhétorique hostile et les menaces géopolitiques<br>• La remise en cause du partage de renseignement |
| **Évaluation globale** | **Pas une menace FIMI systémique à ce stade**, mais un **risque croissant** nécessitant une vigilance accrue et une stratégie de résilience autonome de l'Europe |

Les analystes de l'ISS parlent désormais d'un environnement de **"low trust"** (faible confiance) dans les relations transatlantiques, où l'Europe doit se préparer à **prendre le relais** dans la lutte contre la désinformation, y compris potentiellement contre des acteurs alliés devenus hostiles.

---

**Sources principales consultées :** - EEAS, *3rd Report on Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference (FIMI) Threats*, mars 2025 - EEAS, *2024 Report on EEAS Activities to Counter FIMI*, 2025 - Parlement européen, *Rapport sur les relations politiques entre l'UE et les États-Unis* (A10-0250/2025) - ISS, *Low trust: navigating transatlantic relations under Trump 2.0*, 2025 - EU DisinfoLab, *Documenting the setbacks: The new environment for counter-disinformation in Europe*, 2025 - Presse internationale (The Guardian, Politico, Franceinfo, CNN, Foreign Policy, Washington Post), janvier-juin 2025

#### Lithuanian — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

Based on the research conducted, I provide a detailed answer to your question in Lithuanian:

---

**Is the US a FIMI Threat to Europe?**

**Short answer:** In official EU documents, the United States is **not designated as a FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat actor** in the same context as Russia or China. However, since 2024–2025, concerns have been rapidly growing regarding US interference in European democratic processes, particularly through social media platforms and political support for the far-right.

---

**1. Official EU Position: The US – Partner, Not Enemy**

In the European External Action Service (EEAS) FIMI threat reports (2023–2026), the primary FIMI actors are consistently identified as:

- **Russia** (approx. 29% of attributed incidents)

- **China** (approx. 6% of attributed incidents)

The US is described here as an **ally and partner** in the fight against FIMI. The EU and the US have established a joint methodology for FIMI identification (DISARM framework, STIX 2 standard, OpenCTI platform) through the Trade and Technology Council (TTC).

> "The European Union and the United States have established a close partnership in addressing the issue of information manipulation..." – EEAS documents

---

**2. Recent Developments (2024–2025): Growing Concern**

Despite the official partnership, serious tensions have emerged since late 2024:

**a) US National Security Strategy (December 2025)** The strategy document states that the US will "**cultivate resistance**" in Europe in order to "correct its current trajectory," and praises the growing influence of "patriotic parties." This provoked a sharp reaction:

> "Allies do not threaten to interfere in the domestic political choices of allies... We cannot accept the threat of interference in European political life." – **António Costa**, President of the European Council (December 8, 2025)

**b) US Officials' Interference in Elections** - **JD Vance** (US Vice President) met with the leader of the German far-right AfD, Alice Weidel, in Munich in February 2025, ahead of the elections. Berlin assessed this as **"unacceptable electoral interference."** - **Elon Musk** (owner of X/Twitter, Trump administration ally) publicly supported the AfD in Germany, criticized the annulment of the Romanian elections, and called judges "tyrants."

**c) Influence of Social Media Platforms** EU regulators are investigating whether the X algorithm was manipulated to increase the reach of politicians supported by Musk. The European Commission is conducting investigations against X, Meta, and TikTok under the Digital Services Act (DSA) regarding potential electoral manipulation.

**d) US Criticism of EU Regulation** The Trump administration has criticized the EU Digital Services Act (DSA) as "censorship" and ordered diplomats to run lobbying campaigns against the law. This creates tension regarding the regulation of the information space.

---

**3. Essential Difference: FIMI vs. Political Influence**

It is important to distinguish between two categories:

| **FIMI (as defined by the EU)** | **US Activity in Europe** |
|---|---|
| Covert, coordinated disinformation campaigns | Public political statements |
| Bot networks, fake news pages | Support for specific parties (AfD, Reform UK, National Front) |
| Designated as "Russia" and "China" | Influence of social media platform owners |
| Sanctions, diplomatic measures | Diplomatic pressure, trade threats |

**The US is not officially included in the EU FIMI sanctions lists** like Russia or China. However, in December 2025, the situation is changing – the EU is increasingly discussing "hybrid threats" from various sources, including allies.

---

**4. EU Response: European Democracy Shield**

In November 2025, the European Commission introduced the **European Democracy Shield**, which includes:

- Protection of the integrity of the information space

- Protection of the electoral process from foreign interference

- Strengthening of societal resilience

Official documents of this shield still focus primarily on **Russia**, but it is mentioned that "various actors" may have an interest in undermining EU democracy.

---

**Conclusion**

**At the official level**, the US is not considered a FIMI threat to Europe – they are key partners in the fight against Russian and Chinese disinformation.

**However, in practice**, a new reality has been taking shape since 2024–2025: the US administration and related actors (Elon Musk, JD Vance) are actively interfering in European politics, supporting far-right parties, and criticizing EU regulation. This raises serious questions regarding transatlantic relations and the protection of European democratic processes.

European leaders have already publicly warned Washington against interference, but for now, **there is no official classification of the US into the FIMI threat category** – this remains a diplomatic "grey area" between allied cooperation and unacceptable interference in internal affairs.

Remdamasis atliktais tyrimais, pateikiu išsamų atsakymą į jūsų klausimą lietuvių kalba:

---

**Ar JAV yra FIMI grėsmė Europai?**

**Trumpas atsakymas:** Oficialiose ES dokumentuose Jungtinės Valstijos **nėra įvardijamos kaip FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) grėsmės veikėjos** tame pačiame kontekste kaip Rusija ar Kinija. Tačiau nuo 2024–2025 m. sparčiai auga susirūpinimas dėl JAV įsikišimo į Europos demokratinius procesus, ypač per socialinės žiniasklaidos platformas ir politinę paramą kraštutiniams dešiniesiems.

---

**1. Oficiali ES pozicija: JAV – partneris, ne priešas**

Europos išorės veiksmų tarnybos (EEAS) FIMI grėsmių ataskaitose (2023–2026 m.) nuosekliai kaip pagrindiniai FIMI veikėjai įvardijami:

- **Rusija** (apie 29 % priskirtų incidentų)

- **Kinija** (apie 6 % priskirtų incidentų)

JAV čia aprašomos kaip **sąjungininkė ir partnerė** kovoje su FIMI. ES ir JAV yra sukūrusios bendrą metodologiją FIMI identifikavimui (DISARM sistema, STIX 2 standartas, OpenCTI platforma) per Prekybos ir technologijų tarybą (TTC).

> „Europos Sąjunga ir Jungtinės Valstijos yra sukūrusios glaudžią partnerystę sprendžiant manipuliavimo informacija klausimus..." – EEAS dokumentai

---

**2. Naujausi pokyčiai (2024–2025 m.): augantis susirūpinimas**

Nepaisant oficialios partnerystės, nuo 2024 m. pabaigos išryškėjo rimtų įtampų:

**a) JAV Nacionalinio saugumo strategija (2025 m. gruodis)** Strategijos dokumente teigiama, kad JAV „**kultivuos pasipriešinimą**" Europoje, siekdama „pataisyti jos dabartinę kryptį", ir giria „patriotinių partijų" augančią įtaką. Tai sukėlė aštrią reakciją:

> „Sąjungininkai negraso kištis į sąjungininkų vidaus politinius pasirinkimus... Mes negalime priimti grėsmės kištis į Europos politinį gyvenimą." – **António Costa**, Europos Vadovų Tarybos pirmininkas (2025 m. gruodžio 8 d.)

**b) JAV pareigūnų įsikišimas į rinkimus** - **JD Vance** (JAV viceprezidentas) 2025 m. vasarį Miunchene susitiko su Vokietijos kraštutinių dešiniųjų AfD lyderė Alice Weidel, prieš rinkimus. Berlynas tai įvertino kaip **„nepriimtiną kišimąsi į rinkimus"**. - **Elon Musk** (X/Twitter savininkas, Trump administracijos sąjungininkas) viešai palaikė AfD Vokietijoje, kritikavo Rumunijos rinkimų panaikinimą ir vadino teisėjus „tironais".

**c) Socialinės žiniasklaidos platformų įtaka** ES reguliuotojai tiria, ar X algoritmas nebuvo manipuliuojamas siekiant padidinti Musk palaikomų politikų pasiekiamumą. Europos Komisija atlieka tyrimus prieš X, Meta ir TikTok pagal Skaitmeninių paslaugų aktą (DSA) dėl galimo manipuliavimo rinkimais.

**d) JAV kritika ES reguliavimui** Trumpo administracija kritikuoja ES Skaitmeninių paslaugų aktą (DSA) kaip „censūrą" ir įsakė diplomatams lobistines kampanijas prieš šį įstatymą. Tai sukuria įtampą dėl informacinės erdvės reguliavimo.

---

**3. Esminis skirtumas: FIMI vs. politinė įtaka**

Svarbu atskirti dvi kategorijas:

| **FIMI (kaip apibrėžta ES)** | **JAV veikla Europoje** |
|---|---|
| Slaptos, koordinuotos dezinformacijos kampanijos | Vieši politiniai pareiškimai |
| Botų tinklai, netikri naujienų puslapiai | Parama konkrečioms partijoms (AfD, Reform UK, Nacionalinis frontas) |
| Įvardijama kaip „Rusija" ir „Kinija" | Socialinės žiniasklaidos platformų savininkų įtaka |
| Sankcijos, diplomatinės priemonės | Diplomatinis spaudimas, prekybos grasinimai |

**JAV nėra oficialiai įtrauktos į ES FIMI sankcijų sąrašus** kaip Rusija ar Kinija. Tačiau 2025 m. gruodžio mėn. situacija keičiasi – ES vis aktyviau diskutuoja apie „hibridines grėsmes" iš įvairių šaltinių, įskaitant sąjungininkes.

---

**4. ES atsakas: Europos demokratijos skydas**

2025 m. lapkritį Europos Komisija pristatė **Europos demokratijos skydą** (European Democracy Shield), kuris apima:

- Informacinės erdvės vientisumo apsaugą

- Rinkimų proceso apsaugą nuo užsienio kišimosi

- Visuomenės atsparumo stiprinimą

Oficialiose šio skydo dokumentuose vis dar daugiausia dėmesio skiriama **Rusijai**, tačiau paminėta, kad „įvairūs veikėjai" gali turėti interesą pakenkti ES demokratijai.

---

**Išvada**

**Oficialiame lygmenyje** JAV nėra laikomos FIMI grėsme Europai – jos yra svarbiausios partnerės kovoje su Rusijos ir Kinijos dezinformacija.

**Tačiau praktikoje** nuo 2024–2025 m. formuojasi nauja realybė: JAV administracija ir su ja susiję veikėjai (Elon Musk, JD Vance) aktyviai kišasi į Europos politiką, palaiko kraštutinių dešiniųjų partijas ir kritikuoja ES reguliavimą. Tai kelia rimtų klausimų dėl transatlantinių santykių ir Europos demokratinių procesų apsaugos.

Europos lyderiai jau viešai perspėja Vašingtoną dėl kišimosi, tačiau kol kas **nėra oficialaus JAV priskyrimo FIMI grėsmių kategorijai** – tai išlieka diplomatine „pilkąja zona" tarp sąjungininkų bendradarbiavimo ir nepriimtino kišimosi į vidaus reikalus.

**English translation**

Based on the analysis of official EU documents, EEAS (European External Action Service) reports, and current news from 2024–2026, the following answer can be provided:

**Short Answer**

**Officially, the US is not classified by the European Union as a FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat** in the same sense as Russia or China. However, **in 2025 the Trump administration's actions caused unprecedented concern in Europe** and were characterized by a number of European leaders as unacceptable interference in internal affairs, creating a new dynamic in transatlantic relations.

---

**1. Official EU Position: The US is a Partner, Not a Threat**

In official EEAS reports on FIMI: - The **3rd EEAS Report (March 2025)** and the **4th Report (2026)** explicitly state that the main sources of FIMI for Europe are **Russia (29% of documented incidents)** and **China (6%)**. - The US is mentioned exclusively as a **key partner** in countering FIMI: the EU and the US have developed a joint threat analysis methodology (the DISARM framework), cooperated within the Trade and Technology Council (TTC), and exchanged intelligence.

Prior to 2025, transatlantic cooperation in countering disinformation was considered "exemplary."

---

**2. Sharp Shift in the Situation in 2025**

The Trump administration took a number of steps that European leaders perceived as a threat to democratic processes:

**a) Dismantling US Anti-Disinformation Structures** The US closed key offices dealing with counter-foreign interference: - The **Counter Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference Hub** (State Department) - The **Global Engagement Centre** - The **Foreign Malign Influence Center** (Intelligence Community)

This happened against the backdrop of Russia continuing to step up its FIMI efforts in Europe.

**b) Direct Interference in European Elections** - **Romania (2025)**: Vice President **JD Vance** at the Munich Security Conference (February 2025) criticized the cancellation of the Romanian presidential election, stating that "closing off disfavored views is the surest way to destroy democracy." The US openly demanded that the right-wing candidate Călin Georgescu, supported by Trump, be allowed to run in the election. - **Germany (February 2025)**: Vance met with the leader of the AfD party (classified as extremist) on the eve of the parliamentary elections. Chancellor **Olaf Scholz** called this "direct interference" in democracy: *"We do not accept that outsiders interfere in our democracy, in our elections."* - **Poland (2025)**: Trump personally endorsed right-wing candidate Karol Nawrocki in the presidential election.

**c) New US National Security Strategy (December 2025)** A document published by the White House contains unprecedented criticism of Europe: - Accuses the EU of "undermining democratic processes," "censoring freedom of speech," and "suppressing political opposition." - States that Europe risks "civilizational erasure" due to migration. - Calls on the US to "cultivate resistance" within Europe to "correct its current course." - Openly supports Europe's nationalist right-wing parties.

**Reaction**: European Council President **António Costa** stated: *"Allies do not threaten interference in the internal political elections of their allies. We cannot accept the threat of interference in European political life."*

---

**3. Important Distinction: FIMI vs. Political Pressure from an Ally**

It is necessary to distinguish between two concepts:

| **FIMI (as a threat)** | **US Actions (2025)** |
|---|---|
| Covert operations of hostile states (Russia, China) | Open political pressure from an ally |
| Disinformation, bots, fake media | Public support for parties, diplomatic pressure |
| Goal: undermining democracy | Goal: changing Europe's political course |
| Viewed in EEAS reports as a threat | Not classified as FIMI, but causes a crisis of trust |

Experts (for example, from the EUISS — European Union Institute for Security Studies) note that **the US is rolling back cooperation on countering disinformation**, which weakens the overall transatlantic defense against Russia and China, but **does not make the US a source of FIMI** in the classical sense.

---

**4. Technological Aspect: Pressure on US Platforms**

The EU applies the **Digital Services Act (DSA)** to American tech giants (X, Meta), fining them for inadequate efforts against disinformation. The Trump administration perceives this as "censorship" and "extortion" of American companies, threatening tariffs and restrictions.

This creates tension, but is not qualified as FIMI on the part of the US.

---

**Conclusions**

**Formally**, the US is not included on the list of FIMI threats to Europe — the main threats stem from Russia and China.

**Factically**, an unprecedented crisis occurred in transatlantic relations in 2025: the Trump administration's actions (support for Eurosceptics, election interference, ideological pressure) are perceived by European leaders as **unacceptable foreign interference**, even if it comes from an ally.

**Paradox**: The US, which traditionally has been Europe's main partner in countering FIMI, is now **weakening this fight itself**, shutting down its own structures and criticizing European approaches to disinformation regulation.

**New Reality**: Europe is forced to strengthen its own institutions (European Democracy Shield, FIMI-combating center) without US support, which changes the balance of power in the transatlantic partnership.

Thus, the US **is not a FIMI threat in the classical sense** (like Russia), but its actions in 2025 create a **new type of threat** — political and ideological pressure from an ally, which European leaders consider unacceptable interference in sovereign democratic processes.

**Original text**

На основе анализа официальных документов ЕС, отчетов EEAS (Европейской службы внешних связей) и актуальных новостей 2024–2026 годов, можно дать следующий ответ:

**Краткий ответ**

**Официально США не классифицируются Европейским союзом как угроза FIMI (иностранного манипулирования информацией и вмешательства)** в том же смысле, что Россия или Китай. Однако **в 2025 году действия администрации Трампа вызвали беспрецедентную озабоченность в Европе** и были охарактеризованы рядом европейских лидеров как недопустимое вмешательство во внутренние дела, что создает новую динамику в трансатлантических отношениях.

---

**1. Официальная позиция ЕС: США — партнер, а не угроза**

В официальных отчетах EEAS о FIMI: - **3-й отчет EEAS (март 2025)** и **4-й отчет (2026)** прямо указывают, что основными источниками FIMI для Европы являются **Россия (29% задокументированных инцидентов)** и **Китай (6%)** . - США упоминаются исключительно как **ключевой партнер** в борьбе с FIMI: ЕС и США разработали общую методологию анализа угроз (DISARM framework), сотрудничали в рамках Совета по торговле и технологиям (TTC) и обменивались разведданными .

До 2025 года трансатлантическое сотрудничество в сфере противодействия дезинформации считалось «образцовым».

---

**2. Резкое изменение ситуации в 2025 году**

Администрация Трампа предприняла ряд шагов, которые европейские лидеры восприняли как угрозу демократическим процессам:

**а) Демонтаж американских структур по борьбе с дезинформацией** США закрыли ключевые офисы, занимавшиеся противодействием иностранному вмешательству: - **Counter Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference Hub** (Госдепартамент) - **Global Engagement Centre** - **Foreign Malign Influence Center** (разведывательное сообщество)

Это произошло на фоне того, что Россия продолжает наращивать усилия по FIMI в Европе .

**б) Прямое вмешательство в европейские выборы** - **Румыния (2025)**: Вице-президент **JD Vance** на Мюнхенской конференции безопасности (февраль 2025) раскритиковал отмену президентских выборов в Румынии, заявив, что «закрытие неугодных взглядов — самый верный способ разрушить демократию». США открыто потребовали допустить к выборам правого кандидата Калина Джорджеску, которого поддерживает Трамп . - **Германия (февраль 2025)**: Vance встретился с лидером партии AfD (классифицированной как экстремистская) накануне парламентских выборов. Канцлер **Олаф Шольц** назвал это «прямым вмешательством» в демократию: *«Мы не принимаем, чтобы посторонние вмешивались в нашу демократию, в наши выборы»*. - **Польша (2025)**: Трамп лично поддержал правого кандидата Кароля Навроцкого на президентских выборах.

**в) Новая стратегия национальной безопасности США (декабрь 2025)** Документ, опубликованный Белым домом, содержит беспрецедентную критику Европы: - Обвиняет ЕС в «подрыве демократических процессов», «цензуре свободы слова» и «подавлении политической оппозиции» - Заявляет, что Европа рискует «цивилизационным исчезновением» из-за миграции - Призывает США «культивировать сопротивление» внутри Европы для «коррекции её текущего курса» - Открыто поддерживает националистические правые партии Европы .

**Реакция**: Президент Европейского совета **Антониу Кошта** заявил: *«Союзники не угрожают вмешательством во внутренние политические выборы своих союзников. Мы не можем принять угрозу вмешательства в европейскую политическую жизнь»* .

---

**3. Важное различие: FIMI vs. политическое давление союзника**

Необходимо различать два понятия:

| **FIMI (как угроза)** | **Действия США (2025)** |
|---|---|
| Тайные операции враждебных государств (Россия, Китай) | Открытое политическое давление союзника |
| Дезинформация, боты, фейковые СМИ | Публичная поддержка партий, дипломатическое давление |
| Цель: подрыв демократии | Цель: изменение политического курса Европы |
| Рассматривается в отчетах EEAS как угроза | Не классифицируется как FIMI, но вызывает кризис доверия |

Эксперты (например, из EUISS — Института ЕС по исследованиям в области безопасности) отмечают, что **США сворачивают сотрудничество по борьбе с дезинформацией**, что ослабляет общую трансатлантическую оборону против России и Китая, но **не делают США источником FIMI** в классическом понимании .

---

**4. Технологический аспект: давление на американские платформы**

ЕС применяет **Закон о цифровых услугах (DSA)** к американским технологическим гигантам (X, Meta), штрафуя их за недостаточную борьбу с дезинформацией. Администрация Трампа воспринимает это как «цензуру» и «вымогательство» американских компаний, угрожая тарифами и ограничениями .

Это создает напряженность, но не квалифицируется как FIMI со стороны США.

---

**Выводы**

**Формально** США не включены в список угроз FIMI для Европы — основные угрозы исходят от России и Китая.

**Фактически** в 2025 году произошел беспрецедентный кризис в трансатлантических отношениях: действия администрации Трампа (поддержка евроскептиков, вмешательство в выборы, идеологическое давление) воспринимаются европейскими лидерами как **недопустимое иностранное вмешательство**, даже если оно исходит от союзника.

**Парадокс**: США, которые традиционно были главным партнером Европы в борьбе с FIMI, теперь **сами ослабляют эту борьбу**, закрывая собственные структуры и критикуя европейские подходы к регулированию дезинформации.

**Новая реальность**: Европа вынуждена укреплять собственные институты (Европейский щит демократии, центр по борьбе с FIMI) без поддержки США, что меняет баланс сил в трансатлантическом партнерстве.

Таким образом, США **не являются угрозой FIMI в классическом понимании** (как Россия), но их действия в 2025 году создают **новый тип угрозы** — политическое и идеологическое давление со стороны союзника, которое европейские лидеры считают неприемлемым вмешательством в суверенные демократические процессы.

#### English — answer round 1

*Web access: Used — native search*

*Sources returned by search:* - [Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en) - [https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf](https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf) - [https://www.osce.org/files/f/documents/d/b/593720_0.pdf](https://www.osce.org/files/f/documents/d/b/593720_0.pdf) - [EU drops sponsorship of disinformation conference at Trump officials’ request | Trump administration | The Guardian](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/07/trump-eu-disinformation-conference) - [EU official warns US against European interference | AP News](https://apnews.com/article/dd672c0d0b3f31378c39381ef8ea8dea)

**English translation**

**Yes—as a credible risk Europe should assess, but not as a blanket label for the United States or every American attempt to influence European politics.** The crucial distinction is between *foreign influence*, *political interference*, and the narrower category of *foreign information manipulation and interference*. The EU’s definition makes manipulative conduct—not an actor’s nationality or alliance status—the central test. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**What would make American activity FIMI?**

The European External Action Service (EEAS) defines FIMI as intentional, coordinated, manipulative behaviour that threatens or could negatively affect political processes, procedures, or values. It can involve **state or non-state actors**, and need not be illegal. Consequently, neither being a NATO ally nor being a private American company automatically puts an actor outside the definition. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

But **criticism of European governments, support for a political party, or disagreement over speech regulation is not automatically FIMI**. Applying that definition requires evidence of manipulation and coordination, rather than simply an unwelcome opinion. Examples worth investigating would include concealed sponsorship, deceptive identities, coordinated inauthentic amplification, or deliberate manipulation of information distribution. That is a conduct-based assessment, not a test of political ideology. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Why the concern is credible**

There are concrete reasons to scrutinise U.S. actors:

- **An explicit policy of influencing Europe’s political direction.** The U.S. National Security Strategy, released in December 2025, calls for “cultivating resistance to Europe’s current trajectory within European nations.” This establishes an intention to encourage political change inside European countries. It does **not**, by itself, establish that the means used are manipulative or constitute FIMI. ([whitehouse.gov](https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf))

- **A platform owner’s intervention in an election.** In its report on Germany’s February 23, 2025 election, OSCE/ODIHR documented Elon Musk’s vigorous support for the AfD through X, giving it unprecedented prominence. It also recorded the European Commission’s investigation into X’s recommender system. This warrants scrutiny, but the report does not establish that Musk secretly manipulated the algorithm or acted under U.S. government direction. ([osce.org](https://www.osce.org/files/f/documents/d/b/593720_0.pdf))

- **Pressure on the counter-disinformation community.** On October 7, 2026, the Guardian reported U.S. pressure to withdraw sponsorship of a European disinformation conference—pressure, but not itself proven FIMI. ([theguardian.com](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/07/trump-eu-disinformation-conference))

European Council President António Costa also publicly warned against U.S. interference in European politics on December 8, 2025. That demonstrates an official concern about interference, although it is not equivalent to a technical attribution of a FIMI operation. ([apnews.com](https://apnews.com/article/dd672c0d0b3f31378c39381ef8ea8dea))

**Is this the EU’s official classification?**

**Not in the same sense as its established treatment of Russian and Chinese operations.** The EEAS publicly identifies Russia and China as its particular focus. That focus should not be mistaken for a definition that exempts other countries, but neither should an analytical concern about U.S. actors be presented as an established EU-wide designation. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**My assessment:** Europe should treat American state-linked and private actors as **possible FIMI perpetrators and investigate specific conduct using the same evidentiary standards applied to anyone else**. The evidence supports a credible interference risk; it does not justify calling all American influence FIMI or assuming every private American intervention is a U.S. government operation. An ally should receive neither automatic immunity nor automatic guilt. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Original text**

**Yes—as a credible risk Europe should assess, but not as a blanket label for the United States or every American attempt to influence European politics.** The crucial distinction is between *foreign influence*, *political interference*, and the narrower category of *foreign information manipulation and interference*. The EU’s definition makes manipulative conduct—not an actor’s nationality or alliance status—the central test. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**What would make American activity FIMI?**

The European External Action Service (EEAS) defines FIMI as intentional, coordinated, manipulative behaviour that threatens or could negatively affect political processes, procedures, or values. It can involve **state or non-state actors**, and need not be illegal. Consequently, neither being a NATO ally nor being a private American company automatically puts an actor outside the definition. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

But **criticism of European governments, support for a political party, or disagreement over speech regulation is not automatically FIMI**. Applying that definition requires evidence of manipulation and coordination, rather than simply an unwelcome opinion. Examples worth investigating would include concealed sponsorship, deceptive identities, coordinated inauthentic amplification, or deliberate manipulation of information distribution. That is a conduct-based assessment, not a test of political ideology. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Why the concern is credible**

There are concrete reasons to scrutinise U.S. actors:

- **An explicit policy of influencing Europe’s political direction.** The U.S. National Security Strategy, released in December 2025, calls for “cultivating resistance to Europe’s current trajectory within European nations.” This establishes an intention to encourage political change inside European countries. It does **not**, by itself, establish that the means used are manipulative or constitute FIMI. ([whitehouse.gov](https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf))

- **A platform owner’s intervention in an election.** In its report on Germany’s February 23, 2025 election, OSCE/ODIHR documented Elon Musk’s vigorous support for the AfD through X, giving it unprecedented prominence. It also recorded the European Commission’s investigation into X’s recommender system. This warrants scrutiny, but the report does not establish that Musk secretly manipulated the algorithm or acted under U.S. government direction. ([osce.org](https://www.osce.org/files/f/documents/d/b/593720_0.pdf))

- **Pressure on the counter-disinformation community.** On October 7, 2026, the Guardian reported U.S. pressure to withdraw sponsorship of a European disinformation conference—pressure, but not itself proven FIMI. ([theguardian.com](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/07/trump-eu-disinformation-conference))

European Council President António Costa also publicly warned against U.S. interference in European politics on December 8, 2025. That demonstrates an official concern about interference, although it is not equivalent to a technical attribution of a FIMI operation. ([apnews.com](https://apnews.com/article/dd672c0d0b3f31378c39381ef8ea8dea))

**Is this the EU’s official classification?**

**Not in the same sense as its established treatment of Russian and Chinese operations.** The EEAS publicly identifies Russia and China as its particular focus. That focus should not be mistaken for a definition that exempts other countries, but neither should an analytical concern about U.S. actors be presented as an established EU-wide designation. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**My assessment:** Europe should treat American state-linked and private actors as **possible FIMI perpetrators and investigate specific conduct using the same evidentiary standards applied to anyone else**. The evidence supports a credible interference risk; it does not justify calling all American influence FIMI or assuming every private American intervention is a U.S. government operation. An ally should receive neither automatic immunity nor automatic guilt. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

#### French — answer round 1

*Web access: Used — native search*

*Sources returned by search:* - [https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf) - [INTRODUCTION](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2024/Study%20international%20norms%20on%20FIMI.pdf?utm_source=openai) - [The 2025 US National Security Strategy](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/ATAG/2025/779261/EPRS_ATA%282025%29779261_EN.pdf?utm_source=openai) - [Trump admin bars 5 Europeans it says led drive to "censor" U.S. views online](https://www.axios.com/2025/12/24/trump-immigration-us-state-department-visa-ban-european-tech?utm_source=openai) - [Musk hosts German far-right leader on X, stirs angst about election meddling By Reuters](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/musk-to-host-german-farright-leader-on-x-stirs-angst-about-election-meddling-3804969) - [EU drops sponsorship of disinformation conference at Trump officials’ request | Trump administration | The Guardian](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/07/trump-eu-disinformation-conference) - [Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en?utm_source=openai)

**English translation**

**Yes, certain American actors can represent a FIMI threat to Europe. But broadly qualifying "the United States" as a FIMI actor would be too imprecise: a distinction must be made between the government, companies, individuals, and effectively documented operations.** The available evidence justifies vigilance toward the Trump administration and certain private American actors, without automatically equating their actions to Russian or Chinese operations. ([iss.europa.eu](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf))

**1. What falls — or does not fall — under FIMI**

According to the European External Action Service (EEAS), FIMI refers to **intentional, coordinated, and manipulative** behaviors that are likely to undermine values, procedures, and political processes. They can be carried out by state or non-state actors, and are not necessarily illegal. This definition grants no exemption to allied countries. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2024/Study%20international%20norms%20on%20FIMI.pdf?utm_source=openai))

Conversely, **American criticism of the EU, public political support, or diplomatic pressure are not enough, on their own, to establish a FIMI operation**. The methods must be examined: deception, coordination, concealment, manipulation of dissemination, and harm to democratic debate. The central distinction is therefore between *influence*, *interference*, and *information manipulation*. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2024/Study%20international%20norms%20on%20FIMI.pdf?utm_source=openai))

**2. Why the American risk deserves to be taken seriously**

**An explicit desire to influence the European political trajectory**

The U.S. national security strategy published on **December 5, 2025** plans to "cultivate resistance to Europe's current trajectory within European nations" — a translation of the official text. This is an explicit signal of an intent to exert internal political influence. **However, this does not in itself prove a clandestine or manipulative informational operation.** ([europarl.europa.eu](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/ATAG/2025/779261/EPRS_ATA%282025%29779261_EN.pdf?utm_source=openai))

**Campaigns against European mechanisms to combat disinformation**

An analysis by the EU Institute for Security Studies documents a coordinated campaign by several U.S. diplomatic representations in the spring of 2025, presenting Europe's fight against disinformation as an enterprise of censorship. It also highlights American interventions in favor of European parties and candidates. This constitutes a concerning institutional analysis, **not a formal attribution of FIMI by the EEAS**. ([iss.europa.eu](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf))

On **December 23, 2025**, Washington also imposed entry restrictions on five European figures, including Thierry Breton and officials from organizations working against disinformation or online hatred. Washington invoked the protection of American freedom of speech; France denounced this as pressure on European digital sovereignty. These measures constitute concrete pressure, but are not automatically a FIMI operation. ([axios.com](https://www.axios.com/2025/12/24/trump-immigration-us-state-department-visa-ban-european-tech?utm_source=openai))

**Private actors capable of massively influencing the debate**

On **January 9, 2025**, Elon Musk used X to call on Germans to vote for the AfD. He also relayed the misleading claim that the EU could cancel an electoral victory by this party. This documents a political intervention and an amplification of misleading content; **it is not enough to demonstrate a coordinated operation directed by the U.S. State**, nor an effectively established algorithmic manipulation. ([investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/musk-to-host-german-farright-leader-on-x-stirs-angst-about-election-meddling-3804969))

**3. What is the conclusion for Europe?**

**My assessment is therefore: yes to identifying an American risk; no to a general accusation without precise attribution.** Each operation must be evaluated using the same criteria, regardless of the nationality of its perpetrator.

The distinction is also political: on **October 7, 2026**, *The Guardian* reported that European officials were challenging the qualification of the United States as a disinformation actor. ([theguardian.com](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/07/trump-eu-disinformation-conference))

The right approach would be to protect the European debate against **manipulative behaviors**, while preserving legitimate criticisms and political opinions. In other words: **allied status must not create immunity, but American origin must not become proof of FIMI.** This approach aligns with the behavioral logic of the EEAS framework. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en?utm_source=openai))

**Original text**

**Oui, certains acteurs américains peuvent représenter une menace FIMI pour l’Europe. Mais qualifier globalement « les États-Unis » d’acteur FIMI serait trop imprécis : il faut distinguer le gouvernement, les entreprises, les individus et les opérations effectivement documentées.** Les éléments disponibles justifient une vigilance envers l’administration Trump et certains acteurs privés américains, sans assimiler automatiquement leurs actions aux opérations russes ou chinoises. ([iss.europa.eu](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf))

**1. Ce qui relève — ou non — de la FIMI**

Selon le Service européen pour l’action extérieure (SEAE), la FIMI désigne des comportements **intentionnels, coordonnés et manipulateurs**, susceptibles de porter atteinte aux valeurs, aux procédures et aux processus politiques. Ils peuvent être menés par des acteurs étatiques ou non étatiques, et ne sont pas nécessairement illégaux. Cette définition n’accorde aucune exemption aux pays alliés. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2024/Study%20international%20norms%20on%20FIMI.pdf?utm_source=openai))

En revanche, **une critique américaine de l’UE, un soutien politique public ou une pression diplomatique ne suffisent pas, à eux seuls, à établir une opération FIMI**. Il faut examiner les méthodes : tromperie, coordination, dissimulation, manipulation de la diffusion et atteinte au débat démocratique. La distinction centrale est donc entre *influence*, *ingérence* et *manipulation informationnelle*. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2024/Study%20international%20norms%20on%20FIMI.pdf?utm_source=openai))

**2. Pourquoi le risque américain mérite d’être pris au sérieux**

**Une volonté explicite d’influencer la trajectoire politique européenne**

La stratégie américaine de sécurité nationale publiée le **5 décembre 2025** prévoit de « cultiver la résistance à la trajectoire actuelle de l’Europe au sein des nations européennes » — traduction du texte officiel. C’est un signal explicite de volonté d’influence politique interne. **Cela ne prouve toutefois pas, en soi, une opération informationnelle clandestine ou manipulatrice.** ([europarl.europa.eu](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/RegData/etudes/ATAG/2025/779261/EPRS_ATA%282025%29779261_EN.pdf?utm_source=openai))

**Des campagnes contre les dispositifs européens de lutte contre la désinformation**

Une analyse de l’Institut d’études de sécurité de l’UE documente une campagne coordonnée de plusieurs représentations diplomatiques américaines, au printemps 2025, présentant la lutte européenne contre la désinformation comme une entreprise de censure. Elle souligne aussi les interventions américaines en faveur de partis et candidats européens. Il s’agit d’une analyse institutionnelle préoccupante, **pas d’une attribution formelle de FIMI par le SEAE**. ([iss.europa.eu](https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf))

Le **23 décembre 2025**, Washington a également imposé des restrictions d’entrée à cinq personnalités européennes, dont Thierry Breton et des responsables d’organisations travaillant contre la désinformation ou la haine en ligne. Washington invoquait la protection de la liberté d’expression américaine ; la France a dénoncé une pression sur la souveraineté numérique européenne. Ces mesures constituent une pression concrète, mais ne sont pas automatiquement une opération FIMI. ([axios.com](https://www.axios.com/2025/12/24/trump-immigration-us-state-department-visa-ban-european-tech?utm_source=openai))

**Des acteurs privés capables d’influencer massivement le débat**

Le **9 janvier 2025**, Elon Musk a utilisé X pour appeler les Allemands à voter pour l’AfD. Il a aussi relayé l’affirmation trompeuse selon laquelle l’UE pourrait annuler une victoire électorale de ce parti. Cela documente une intervention politique et une amplification de contenus trompeurs ; **cela ne suffit pas à démontrer une opération coordonnée dirigée par l’État américain**, ni une manipulation algorithmique effectivement établie. ([investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/musk-to-host-german-farright-leader-on-x-stirs-angst-about-election-meddling-3804969))

**3. Quelle conclusion pour l’Europe ?**

**Mon appréciation est donc : oui à l’identification d’un risque américain ; non à une accusation générale sans attribution précise.** Il faut évaluer chaque opération selon les mêmes critères, quelle que soit la nationalité de son auteur.

La distinction est aussi politique : le **7 octobre 2026**, le *Guardian* rapportait que des responsables européens contestaient la qualification des États-Unis comme acteur de désinformation. ([theguardian.com](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/07/trump-eu-disinformation-conference))

La bonne approche serait de protéger le débat européen contre **les comportements manipulateurs**, tout en préservant les critiques légitimes et les opinions politiques. Autrement dit : **le statut d’allié ne doit pas créer d’immunité, mais la provenance américaine ne doit pas devenir une preuve de FIMI.** Cette approche correspond à la logique comportementale du cadre du SEAE. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en?utm_source=openai))

#### Lithuanian — answer round 1

*Web access: Used — native search*

*Sources returned by search:* - [Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en) - [Musk uses X livestream to amplify German far-right leader's views ahead of an election | AP News](https://apnews.com/article/521a8fb77250ee0f354fa4e76c1ce63d) - [Vance meets German far-right AfD leader amid election interference criticism By Reuters](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/berlin-says-vance-should-not-interfere-in-german-politics-3870229?utm_source=openai) - [The 2025 US National Security Strategy | Think Tank | European Parliament](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/thinktank/en/document/EPRS_ATA%282025%29779261?utm_source=openai)

**English translation**

**Yes, actors of U.S. origin can pose a FIMI threat to Europe, but it would be inaccurate to automatically label the entire U.S. state or any of its political influence as FIMI.** In my view, public facts substantiate concern regarding interference, but specific information manipulation operations must be proven on a case-by-case basis. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**What is considered FIMI?**

The European External Action Service (EEAS) defines FIMI as **intentional, coordinated, manipulative behavior** that threatens values, procedures, and political processes. It can be carried out by both state and non-state actors. Such activity is not necessarily illegal, and the focus of the assessment is not only on false content, but also on the techniques of manipulation. The definition is not limited to Russia or China. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

Therefore, applying this definition, the same criteria should apply to U.S. actors: **it matters what they do and how they do it, rather than just what country they are from.** However, public criticism, diplomatic disagreement, or openly expressed support for a party do not in themselves prove FIMI. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Why is concern regarding U.S. actors substantiated?**

- **Elon Musk’s involvement in the German elections.** On January 9, 2025, he conversed on the "X" platform with Alice Weidel, leader of the "Alternative for Germany" (AfD) party, and urged people to vote for this party. This is a documented attempt to influence foreign voters' choices. Nevertheless, this conversation alone does not prove covert coordination or algorithmic manipulation; the European Commission also acknowledged his right to express an opinion. ([apnews.com](https://apnews.com/article/521a8fb77250ee0f354fa4e76c1ce63d))

- **Political engagement by the U.S. administration.** On February 14, 2025, Vice President J. D. Vance met with Weidel and criticized other parties for distancing themselves from the AfD. German officials viewed this as unwanted interference in elections. This case substantiates concern regarding political interference, but in itself is not yet proof of a coordinated FIMI operation. ([investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/berlin-says-vance-should-not-interfere-in-german-politics-3870229?utm_source=openai))

- **Officially declared policy direction.** The U.S. National Security Strategy published on December 5, 2025, outlines the promotion of resistance to the current European direction within European states. The European Parliamentary Research Service links this to an orientation toward ideologically aligned parties. In my view, this is a significant signal of interference risk, although strategic intent does not yet prove a specific manipulation operation. ([europarl.europa.eu](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/thinktank/en/document/EPRS_ATA%282025%29779261?utm_source=openai))

**What conclusion would be most accurate?**

**It is reasonable for Europe to assess both U.S. state and private actors as potential sources of FIMI threats — without applying an exemption to allies.** However, a distinction must be made between the actions of the U.S. administration, the activities of private individuals, and platform decisions: American origin alone does not prove direction by the U.S. government. Evidence of intent, coordination, and manipulative behavior is essential for a specific attribution of FIMI. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

At the same time, it should not be claimed that the scale of threats or the evidentiary base are already identical: the EEAS public threat assessment continues to pay special attention to Russia and China, while the March 2026 report documents systematic Russian operations against Europe. **This does not negate the risk of U.S. origin, but it prevents all actors from being automatically equated.** ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Original text**

**Taip, JAV kilmės veikėjai gali kelti FIMI grėsmę Europai, tačiau būtų netikslu visą JAV valstybę ar bet kokią jos politinę įtaką automatiškai vadinti FIMI.** Mano vertinimu, vieši faktai pagrindžia susirūpinimą dėl kišimosi, bet konkrečias informacinio manipuliavimo operacijas reikia įrodyti atskirai. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Kas laikoma FIMI?**

Europos išorės veiksmų tarnyba (EIVT) FIMI apibrėžia kaip **tyčinį, koordinuotą, manipuliacinį elgesį**, kuris kelia grėsmę vertybėms, procedūroms ir politiniams procesams. Jį gali vykdyti tiek valstybės, tiek nevalstybiniai veikėjai. Tokia veikla nebūtinai yra neteisėta, o vertinimo centre yra ne vien melagingas turinys, bet ir manipuliavimo būdai. Apibrėžimas nėra apribotas Rusija ar Kinija. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

Todėl, taikant šį apibrėžimą, JAV veikėjams turėtų galioti tie patys kriterijai: **svarbu, ką ir kaip jie daro, o ne vien tai, iš kokios šalies yra.** Tačiau vieša kritika, diplomatinis nesutarimas ar atvirai išreikšta parama partijai savaime dar neįrodo FIMI. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

**Kodėl susirūpinimas JAV veikėjais pagrįstas?**

- **Elono Musko įsitraukimas į Vokietijos rinkimus.** 2025 m. sausio 9 d. jis platformoje „X“ kalbėjosi su „Alternatyvos Vokietijai“ (AfD) lydere Alice Weidel ir ragino balsuoti už šią partiją. Tai dokumentuotas bandymas paveikti užsienio rinkėjų pasirinkimą. Vis dėlto vien šis pokalbis neįrodo slapto koordinavimo ar manipuliavimo algoritmais; Europos Komisija taip pat pripažino jo teisę reikšti nuomonę. ([apnews.com](https://apnews.com/article/521a8fb77250ee0f354fa4e76c1ce63d))

- **JAV administracijos politinis įsitraukimas.** 2025 m. vasario 14 d. viceprezidentas J. D. Vance’as susitiko su Weidel ir kritikavo kitų partijų atsiribojimą nuo AfD. Vokietijos pareigūnai tai vertino kaip nepageidaujamą kišimąsi į rinkimus. Šis atvejis pagrindžia susirūpinimą politiniu kišimusi, tačiau savaime dar nėra įrodymas apie koordinuotą FIMI operaciją. ([investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/berlin-says-vance-should-not-interfere-in-german-politics-3870229?utm_source=openai))

- **Oficialiai deklaruota politikos kryptis.** 2025 m. gruodžio 5 d. paskelbtoje JAV nacionalinio saugumo strategijoje numatytas pasipriešinimo dabartinei Europos krypčiai skatinimas Europos valstybėse. Europos Parlamento tyrimų tarnyba tai sieja su orientacija į ideologiškai artimas partijas. Mano vertinimu, tai reikšmingas kišimosi rizikos signalas, nors strateginis ketinimas dar neįrodo konkrečios manipuliavimo operacijos. ([europarl.europa.eu](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/thinktank/en/document/EPRS_ATA%282025%29779261?utm_source=openai))

**Kokia išvada būtų tiksliausia?**

**Europai pagrįsta vertinti ir JAV valstybinius bei privačius veikėjus kaip galimus FIMI grėsmės šaltinius — netaikant sąjungininkams išimties.** Tačiau reikia atskirti JAV administracijos veiksmus, privačių asmenų veiklą ir platformų sprendimus: vien amerikietiška kilmė neįrodo JAV valdžios vadovavimo. Konkrečiam FIMI priskyrimui būtini tyčios, koordinavimo ir manipuliacinio elgesio įrodymai. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

Kartu nereikėtų teigti, kad grėsmių mastas ar įrodymų bazė jau yra vienodi: EIVT viešame grėsmių vertinime ypatingas dėmesys tebėra skiriamas Rusijai ir Kinijai, o 2026 m. kovo ataskaita dokumentuoja sistemines Rusijos operacijas prieš Europą. **Tai nepaneigia JAV kilmės rizikos, bet neleidžia visų veikėjų automatiškai sulyginti.** ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/information-integrity-and-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-interference-fimi_en))

#### Russian — answer round 1

*Web access: Used — native search*

*Sources returned by search:* - [Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803) - [https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf](https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf) - [Musk hosts German far-right leader on X, stirs angst about election meddling By Reuters](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/musk-to-host-german-farright-leader-on-x-stirs-angst-about-election-meddling-3804969) - [Keynote speech by HRVP Kaja Kallas at the 2026 Conference on Countering Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference: ‘From Insight to Impact’ | EEAS](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en)

**English translation**

**Yes — there are grounds to consider certain American state and non-state actors as a source of FIMI risk for Europe. But the assertion that "the US as a whole is a proven FIMI threat" is too broad:** it is necessary to distinguish political influence from manipulative operations and to evaluate specific actions. European methodology allows for such an assessment regardless of the country of origin of the actor. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

**What is considered FIMI**

According to the definition by the European External Action Service (EEAS), FIMI is **intentional, coordinated, and manipulative activity** that threatens political processes, procedures, and values. It is not necessarily illegal, and its participants can include states, private organizations, individuals, and intermediaries. Therefore, the allied status of the US does not in itself exclude the possibility of FIMI. The latter is a deduction from the definition, rather than an official designation of the US as a hostile state. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

At the same time, **EU criticism, interviews with a European politician, or public support for a party do not in themselves prove FIMI**: it is necessary to establish the manipulative nature, intentionality, and coordination of actions. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

**Why concerns are justified**

**1. Official American strategy provides for impacting Europe's domestic politics.**

The 2025 US National Security Strategy explicitly names promoting resistance to the current European political course within European countries as a priority. This is evidence of a stated intention to influence European politics, although in itself it does not prove the execution of a specific manipulative operation. In a resolution dated **January 21, 2026**, the European Parliament characterized the actions envisaged by the strategy as resembling foreign interference. ([whitehouse.gov](https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf))

**2. There are concrete examples of American influence on European elections.**

On **January 9, 2025**, Elon Musk held a conversation on the platform X, which he owns, with Alice Weidel and urged Germans to vote for the "Alternative for Germany" ahead of the February 23 elections. This is a documented example of foreign political support. However, to qualify as FIMI, additional evidence of manipulative mechanisms is needed; Musk's actions also cannot be automatically attributed to the US government. ([investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/musk-to-host-german-farright-leader-on-x-stirs-angst-about-election-meddling-3804969))

**3. Control over platforms creates a separate risk.**

At a conference on FIMI on **March 17, 2026**, Kaja Kallas pointed out the problem of large platforms influenced by actors who openly support certain political groups, and raised the issue of the dependence of the European democratic space on American and Chinese companies. This is a warning about structural vulnerability, not proof that any American platform is conducting a state-level interference operation. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en))

**What limits this conclusion**

European assessments continue to single out primarily **Russia and China**; in a resolution dated **September 16, 2026**, the European Parliament named Russia the most serious state source of hybrid threats to the EU. Therefore, equating American risks with the scale and nature of Russian activity without additional evidence is incorrect. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

In addition, the reduction of American work against foreign interference weakens overall defense — Kallas separately noted this in March 2026. But **refusing to help counter FIMI and independently carrying out FIMI are different things**. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en))

**Summary:** It is justified for Europe to take American actors into account in assessing FIMI threats, without granting them an exemption based on allied relations. However, the most accurate phrasing is **"risks of interference by specific American actors exist,"** rather than "the entire US is a single source of FIMI." The criterion should be proven methods and actions, rather than nationality or political views. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

**Original text**

**Да — есть основания считать отдельных американских государственных и негосударственных акторов источником риска FIMI для Европы. Но утверждение «США в целом являются доказанной FIMI-угрозой» слишком широкое:** необходимо отличать политическое влияние от манипулятивных операций и оценивать конкретные действия. Европейская методология допускает такую оценку независимо от страны происхождения актора. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

**Что считается FIMI**

По определению Европейской службы внешних связей (EEAS), FIMI — это **намеренная, скоординированная и манипулятивная деятельность**, угрожающая политическим процессам, процедурам и ценностям. Она необязательно незаконна, а ее участниками могут быть государства, частные организации, отдельные лица и посредники. Поэтому союзнический статус США сам по себе не исключает возможности FIMI. Последнее — вывод из определения, а не официальное обозначение США как враждебного государства. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

При этом **критика ЕС, интервью с европейским политиком или публичная поддержка партии сами по себе еще не доказывают FIMI**: необходимо установить манипулятивный характер, намеренность и координацию действий. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

**Почему опасения обоснованны**

**1. Официальная американская стратегия предусматривает воздействие на внутреннюю политику Европы.**

Стратегия национальной безопасности США 2025 года прямо называет приоритетом содействие сопротивлению нынешнему политическому курсу Европы внутри европейских стран. Это свидетельство заявленного намерения влиять на европейскую политику, хотя само по себе оно не доказывает проведения конкретной манипулятивной операции. Европарламент в резолюции от **21 января 2026 года** охарактеризовал предусмотренные стратегией действия как сходные с иностранным вмешательством. ([whitehouse.gov](https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf))

**2. Есть конкретные примеры американского влияния на европейские выборы.**

**9 января 2025 года** Илон Маск провел на принадлежащей ему платформе X беседу с Алисой Вайдель и призвал немцев голосовать за «Альтернативу для Германии» перед выборами 23 февраля. Это документированный пример иностранной политической поддержки. Однако для квалификации как FIMI нужны дополнительные доказательства манипулятивных механизмов; действия Маска также нельзя автоматически приписывать правительству США. ([investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/world-news/musk-to-host-german-farright-leader-on-x-stirs-angst-about-election-meddling-3804969))

**3. Контроль над платформами создает отдельный риск.**

На конференции по FIMI **17 марта 2026 года** Кая Каллас указала на проблему крупных платформ, на которые влияют акторы, открыто поддерживающие определенные политические группы, и поставила вопрос о зависимости европейского демократического пространства от американских и китайских компаний. Это предупреждение о структурной уязвимости, а не доказательство того, что любая американская платформа ведет государственную операцию вмешательства. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en))

**Что ограничивает такой вывод**

Европейские оценки по-прежнему выделяют прежде всего **Россию и Китай**; в резолюции от **16 сентября 2026 года** Европарламент назвал Россию наиболее серьезным государственным источником гибридных угроз для ЕС. Поэтому приравнивать американские риски к масштабу и характеру российской деятельности без дополнительных доказательств некорректно. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

Кроме того, сокращение американской работы против иностранного вмешательства ослабляет общую защиту — Каллас отдельно отметила это в марте 2026 года. Но **отказ помогать противодействию FIMI и самостоятельное проведение FIMI — разные вещи**. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/keynote-speech-hrvp-kaja-kallas-2026-conference-countering-foreign-information-manipulation-and_en))

**Итог:** Европe обоснованно учитывать американских акторов в оценке угроз FIMI, не предоставляя им исключение на основании союзнических отношений. Но наиболее точная формулировка — **«существуют риски вмешательства со стороны конкретных американских акторов»**, а не «все США являются единым источником FIMI». Критерием должны быть доказанные методы и действия, а не национальность или политические взгляды. ([eeas.europa.eu](https://www.eeas.europa.eu/eeas/tackling-disinformation-foreign-information-manipulation-interference_en?s=2803))

#### English — answer round 1
